# OreHeart — Build Spec (Phase 1B, Round 26 — owner edits, model clips, failing checks)

## Summary

**Phase 1B Round 26 (this build)** removes the owner-rejected Round 24 skull throw and applies the owner's Oct 28 edits: a card drag centered on the pointer that shrinks onto the square under it, native-resolution full screen, Flow-then-Bank paying with a "Use your banked crystals?" question, smaller Commander speech bubbles, a fully visible pickaxe badge, a Done drop-up, faction emblems on owned mines, a bigger Respond window, a hand-placed Junk Shaman lift and a hover-flicker fix. Models play their OH_ clips by name and carry their parts exactly as in the owner's Model Editor. Read "Round 26" first; Rounds 24 and 25 are discontinued.

**Phase 1B Round 24 (discontinued, removed in Round 26)** gives Skullflinger (`goblin-slinger`) the owner's new `Skull_Throw` attack. A real skull leaves his right hand at the time given in `events.json`, flies to the target in a short spinning arc, bonks off it with a small cartoon impact, bounces once and fades away. No rule or number changes. It also re-checks the Round 23 work and the checks that still failed.

**Phase 1B Round 23 (this build)** makes the in-match hand fan exactly like the owner's layout editor (spacing, angle, arc, pop), tucks it after a grab and whenever the mouse leaves it, fixes the remaining real failures (Tutorial Skip, Mine Control stance, Card Comp watch bar, Bowler Brawler check), lets flying units hover, and gives the new Gnome and Dwarf models their own heights. Read "Round 23" under Pages & features first.

Oreheart is a comic-style fantasy card battle in the browser. Goblins, Gnomes and Dwarves fight over three randomly placed crystal mines on a hidden 13×7 grid, and you win by bringing the enemy Commander from 20 Health to 0. Phase 1a delivers **one complete, playable match against a computer opponent (Easy, Normal or Hard) plus a guided tutorial match and a testing deck builder**. It is built on a pure, data-driven rules engine that could later run on a server, on a **landscape** screen. **Phase 1B Round 1** added finished dark-fantasy art for the Goblins, Grubnak, all Strongholds, the battlefield, mines, menu, logo and card design, plus sound effects, without changing any rule or layout (see the Phase 1B section first). **Phase 1B Round 2 (Build A)** reworks the cards on the owner's frames (short text, tap for more), screens (title, select, mulligan with 3 mulligans), the diagonal battlefield camera, grouped HUD, reveals, visible spell steps, zoom/pan and the Deck Builder, and opens the Respond window only when something is playable (read the Round 2 section first). **Phase 1B Round 3a** turned the camera straight-on with road perspective, moved the Strongholds to opposite corners (player row 7 column 2, enemy row 1 column 12) with corner-mirrored mines, allowed decks of 50–100 cards with up to 20 Crystal cards, and added nine starter decks. **Phase 1B Round 3b** polished the cards (gold numbers, rarity crystals, names and text), the Deck Builder, every control and scrollbar, the title screen, spell visuals and tutorial text, with no rule changes. **Phase 1B Round 4** doubled the mulligan cards, rebuilds the nine starter decks at 100 cards, adds faction-locked spells (with the new Venom Shiv poison) on new spell frames, and switched text to metal lettering. **Phase 1B Round 5a (this build)** replaces that with one plain gold font (Alegreya SC Italic), makes menu buttons plain glowing text, makes every menu screen fill the window, redesigns the Deck Builder (deck carousel, big name and list), adds Remove/Restore/Copy for starter decks, enlarges Choose Your Commander and installs the owner's real spell frames (read the Round 5a section first). Rules, numbers and cards come from `factory-design/design/oreheart-gdd-v2.md` (the GDD). Scope comes from `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1a-brief.md` (the brief, including its "Revision 9", "Revision 8", "Revision 7", "Revision 6", "Revision 5", "Revision 4", "Revision 3" and "Revision 2" sections). Where this spec and the GDD disagree, **this spec wins**, because it settles GDD gaps for testing.

**Phase 1B Round 5b** rebuilt the match screen to the owner's own camera numbers and HUD layout. **Phase 1B Round 6a** (this build) fixes the owner's playtest findings: Done moves bottom-right, the hand is 2× and tucked halfway, a 2× top-layer pop-out, an original Oreheart card back, enemy hand centered, a shared text palette, Deck Builder close-up and "+" fixes, no corner ornaments and the owner's glTF mine model; rules are unchanged. **Phase 1B Round 7** (this build) fixes the stuck hand pop-out, shrinks the hand cards ~10 %, uses the owner's card back and deck-pile art exactly, fixes the popped-card glow, sets the mine to exactly 1.5 tall, and (Build B) replaces Grubnak with a Meshy 3D model with animations and a 3D viewer. **Phase 1B Round 8a** (this build) replaces the match board with the owner's painted Skullfang Arena (swamp background, arena painting, all 54 effects, foreground cut-outs, squares mapped onto the painted floor by homography, no grid lines), adds new view controls (zoom, pan, Recenter, cover-fit with no black bars, never stretched) and replaces the mine with a Meshy 3D crystal mine (Round 8a, approved). **Phase 1B Round 8b** (this build) makes the player's Commander face the opponent with look-back and impatience emotes, flips the card glow, tightens the hand hover box, shrinks and smooths the pop-out, replaces the stat bars with small painted icons, fixes Deck Builder dragging and layout shift, reworks Choose Your Commander (uniform decks, focused Commander with his 3D model, only his decks, Fight! bottom-right), turns body text brown-gold and adds a 3D viewer to the Deck Builder close-up. **Phase 1B Round 9a** (this build) rebuilds the match screen exactly to the owner's layout file with the Goblin theme (faction themes as data), separate bank pieces, a drop-down Menu, the editor's hand motion, owner piece sizes with Grubnak holding his scepter, code-built 3D crystal mines, Crystal Flow shard streams and music (Round 9a). **Phase 1B Round 9c** (this build) fixes the arena that never drew, poses the Commanders on the ground inside their strongholds facing the owner's `turn`, builds every menu screen from the owner's Menu Builder file (new Intro screen, new main menu buttons and picture), and adds Deck Builder items (faction colors, 3D viewer for every creature, double-click to play, Play action, white text). Read the Round 9c section first; Round 9b, the in-game editor, is not built yet.

**Phase 1B Round 10** applies the owner's 9c playtest: brighter faction-colored drag highlights (red for attacks), played cards popping up at the screen edges in stacks, mines that may touch and are clickable as a whole, four rule changes (a dying basic Miner draws a card, Ranged shoots in 8 straight lines up to 5 squares, Crystal cards any time, Tunnel any time), Crystal Flow into the HUD crystal counters, a twice-as-large card close-up, a quarter-tucked hand, and flat, level mines with a ground glow instead of a ring.

**Phase 1B Round 11** (this build) applies the owner's round 10 playtest: a "Click to begin" gate so the intro sounds and continuous menu music really play, main menu text exactly as the owner's menus file, glowing edge-light square highlights and unit rings with their own Settings panels, mine clicks limited to the mine's own square, opponent-only card pop-ups, a draggable/reorderable hand that shows the real card while dragging, five fixes, and the Ranged range raised to 7 (still straight lines, blocked by the first unit). Where older sections say Ranged range 5, R11.17 wins.

**Phase 1B Round 12a** (this build) fixes the blocker that dragging a hand card onto the board never played it, lays the raised hand out as an even fan that never covers the board, and installs the owner's own card paintings for Grubgrabber Miner, Boneknocker, Shiv Rat and Scrapsmith Miner plus Grubnak's on-throne portrait. No 3D models, Meshy or throne this round (Round 12b).

**Phase 1B Round 12b-1** (this build) replaces the figures of Grubgrabber Miner, Boneknocker, Shiv Rat and Scrapsmith Miner with rigged Meshy 3D models made once from the owner's sheets (with their gear and animations), and seats a new Grubnak model on his junk throne, which replaces the Goblin castle/stand. Rules and layout are unchanged. Where older sections describe Grubnak standing in/on his fortress with a separate scepter (R7.B3, R9a, R9c.2), R12b.5 wins once the seated model is delivered.

**Phase 1B Round 12c** (this build) makes every card do what the owner's card text in `cards.json` says (Goblins, Gnomes, Dwarves, neutral spells and Crystal cards), adds per-card Ranged ranges, `artPos` picture framing and full-size Crystal pictures, and archives Betty Fizzlebottom. Where an older section conflicts with a card's own text, R12c wins.

**Phase 1B Round 12d** draws every card face in the game (hand, close-ups, Deck Builder, menus, played-card pop-ups, Choose Your Commander, card backs) with the owner's binding card format ("Card format final 1.0") and the owner's own renderer, and deletes the old frame art. Rules, abilities and layout are unchanged.

**Phase 1B Round 13** removes the dark boxes from spell faces, applies the owner's picture framing (`artPos`) on every card face, adds clickable ability buttons above units with activated abilities, gives the Deck Builder three Commander portraits by the title (cards the Commander can't use are hidden, not locked) and a tidy top bar, replaces the title-screen logo with a fanned stack of card backs, and cleans up outdated checks. Rules are unchanged.

**Phase 1B Round 14** makes the match screen match the owner's Oct 24 Game Editor layout exactly (positions and text style), fans both hands, sends Crystal Flow shards to each side's Flow crystal with more shards at higher Flow, makes Goblin Rocket Rider a Fighter and builds Corpsman Brewlord's heal/save ability with the Drunk state.

**Phase 1B Round 16** added no new features: it finishes Round 15 and makes every failing check pass (329 of 399 passed in Round 15). Order: Round 15 items, the hand, Crystal Flow, card abilities and rules, then everything else (see "Phase 1B, Round 16" below).

**Phase 1B Round 15** first makes every Round 14 item actually work (its checks never finished; about 100 checks failed because the test hooks were unreachable), then: units keep facing the way they last moved or acted, Bowler Brawler's Strike rolls a bowling ball that scatters creatures sideways like pins, all text outside card faces is plain white, and every unit gets the owner's new stat badge.

**This build (Round 22):** fixes the 31 failing checks (with the owner's Oct 25 control and hand decisions: left-click selects, right-click opens a unit's card, closer hand fan with a Play this card / Cast this spell button), gives Bowler Brawler his bowling throw, finger spin and basketball shot with a green-flame ball, and adds the owner's Crystal Node effects in each owner's color, while keeping the build under 95 MB.

**Round 21 (built):** every goblin unit appears on the board as its real 3D model. The 12 finished Meshy models in `factory-design/models/` are copied into the game (compressed), items are attached exactly as in the owner's saved looks, and each model uses its own idle/walk/attack/hit/defeat (and mine) clips. Nothing else changes. Read "Round 21" below first.

**Round 17 finish (built):** Round 17 is built (379 of 411 checks passed). This run keeps everything, adopts the owner's own Oct 24 code changes as binding (bonus draws, white unowned mines, no yellow ring under units, smaller stat badge, owner pop-ups/speech bubbles/banners/end pictures, Back bottom-left), and fixes the remaining failing checks. Read "Round 17 finish" below first.

**Phase 1B Round 17** first fixes Round 16's failures (one visible **Fight!** button, the "Your hand" fan fully on screen at rest, test hooks only in test mode, colored mines, the rest of the failing list), then adds three game modes on Choose Your Commander — **Standard** (unchanged), **Card Play** (you only play cards; your units act on their own following one of four stance buttons: Aggressive, Mine Control, Guard, Free-for-all) and **Card Comp vs Card Comp** (the computer plays both sides with your deck while you watch). Read the Round 17 section first.

Tech: Vite + TypeScript (vanilla or React), Three.js for the 3D scene, static `dist/` output, no backend.

### Round 26 (owner edits, model clips, failing checks) — READ FIRST

Source: `factory-design/design/oreheart-round-26-combined-brief.md` (binding; the whole job). **Rounds 24 and 25 are discontinued**: do not build them from any older text. Change nothing that is not listed here: no new rules, no new or changed card numbers, no new cards, no renames. Keep `dist/` under 95 MB (AC90; it is 92.2 MB); shrink only textures, never model geometry or UVs. Owner art is binding (never invent pictures). List every value chosen or changed below in `REPORT.md` under **"Editor updates needed"**, plus the final `dist/` size. Priority if the budget runs out: R26.0 → A1, A2, A6, A3, A8, A10 → A4, A5, A7, A9 → B → C. REPORT.md lists each item as Done / Partly / Not started.

- **R26.0 Remove the Round 24 skull throw (first).** Undo only the skull-throw parts of commit `7018719` (`creature3d.ts`, `scene.ts`, `style.css`, `BUILD_NOTES.md` notes). Keep the later commits (`da7b348`, `b37b320`, `c5a3242`, `63e00e6`). Skullflinger attacks with his normal attack clip (`OH_Attack` if his `clips` map names it, otherwise `Skull_Throw`); no projectile object is created (`getRenderStats().projectiles` / `projectileLog` have no entry for him); the hit still lands for exactly his Attack. (AC391, AC396)
- **R26.A1 Card drag.** While a hand card is dragged (mouse or touch) a floating copy with `aria-label="Dragged card"` follows the pointer with the pointer at its **exact center** for the whole drag (no corner-hang, no 24° lean; small pick-up pop, tilt ≤ 8° around the center). Over a board square it shrinks in ~150 ms to that square's on-screen shape (a `matrix3d` from the card box onto `squareQuad(row,col)`), centered on the pointer (≥ 85 % opacity allowed); between squares it keeps the nearest square's shape; back over the hand or off the board it grows back to its held size. The square under it gets a bright outline above the board: normal highlight color if legal, muted red if not. Dropping, reordering, tap-to-enlarge, spells, Crystal cards and Smith swaps work as before, also on touch and Graphics: Low. `owner-layout.json` hand `overlap` becomes **0.26** (still read from the file, still squeezing to fit). New test hook `window.oreheart.getDragCard()` → `null` when nothing is dragged, else `{ cardId, cx, cy, width, height, corners: [{x,y}×4] (current on-screen corners, window CSS px), opacity, highlight: { row, col, legal } | null }`. (AC370–AC374, AC363)
- **R26.A2 Full screen, native resolution.** The 3D renderer uses `setPixelRatio(devicePixelRatio)` (capped at 2 only on Graphics: Low) and resizes on window/fullscreen changes; 2D canvases also use `devicePixelRatio`. No scroll bars at any size; keep the 16:9 computer letterbox. `getRenderStats().canvas = { cssWidth, cssHeight, bufferWidth, bufferHeight, pixelRatio }` for the 3D canvas. The **first click/tap anywhere** in a visit calls `document.documentElement.requestFullscreen()` once (errors/refusals ignored silently). (AC375, AC376)
- **R26.A3 Paying.** Every payment takes Flow first, then Bank. If a player payment needs any Bank crystals, a pop-up `role="dialog"` named **"Use your banked crystals?"** appears with **Yes** / **No** buttons and a **"Don't ask again"** checkbox. Yes pays and plays; No cancels (card back in hand, nothing spent). "Don't ask again" + Yes turns the setting off. Settings gets a toggle button **"Ask before using banked crystals: On"** / **"...: Off"** (default On, saved in localStorage like the other settings). The AI, `window.oreheart.act` and test mode (`window.__app.test = true`) never show it. (AC377–AC379)
- **R26.A4 Commander speech bubbles ~35 % smaller** (scale 0.65, same anchor); unit bubbles unchanged. The bubble has `aria-label="Commander speech"`. Test hooks: `window.oreheart.showCommanderSpeech(side, text)` shows a bubble; `window.oreheart.getUiScales()` → `{ commanderSpeech: { scale, unscaledWidth }, respond: { scale, unscaledWidth, unscaledHeight } }` where `unscaled*` = the size the currently shown element would have at the previous build's size (scale 1). (AC380)
- **R26.A5 Mine-all pickaxe badge** (the `×N` badge on a mine's site button, now marked with attribute `data-mine-badge`) is fully inside the window and not covered or clipped by anything at every size. (AC381)
- **R26.A6 Done.** If the player made any move this phase (card, move, attack, mine, ability), Done ends the phase with no question. If no move was made and moves remain, a drop-up opens upward from the Done button (bottom edge 0–12 px above Done's top edge), styled like the Match menu pop-up (same background color — see-through dark — and black border, font, buttons): `role="dialog"`, text **"Moves still available (miners haven't mined). Continue to the next phase?"**, buttons **Continue** (ends the phase) and **Go back** (closes it); clicking outside closes it. No moves possible → no question. Replaces the old centered dialog; "Moves reminder: Off" still disables it. (AC382–AC384, AC31, AC37, AC41)
- **R26.A7 Mine emblem.** A controlled mine's site button shows the owning faction's emblem `<img>` from `public/art/card-format/format.json` (layer "Prismatic Crystal World Emblem" `byFaction`: goblins `images/2298e4af.webp`, gnomes `images/42e54bde.webp`, dwarves `images/e56e3c50.webp`), right after a mining action changes the owner. No 💀/⚙️/🔨 emoji on mines. Unowned mines unchanged. (AC385)
- **R26.A8 Respond window 1.3×** box (1.25–1.35× old width and height), text ~1.3×, every button ≥ 44 px tall with clear gaps; at 844×390 the box and every button fit on screen without page scroll. Timer and logic unchanged. (AC386)
- **R26.A9 Junk Shaman lift.** Clicking his `pickUp` ability button, then the character to lift, starts lift mode: the character follows the pointer slightly above the board, the legal landing squares (exactly the engine's legal `pickUp` destinations: empty, within 5 of the Shaman, allowed for that unit) are highlighted, the square under the pointer gets the strong highlight. Clicking a highlighted square drops it there and pays 2 (Flow first, A3). Illegal squares do nothing; Esc, right-click or clicking the Shaman cancels with nothing paid. The AI keeps `bestShaman`. Hook `window.oreheart.getLiftState()` → `{ active, shamanUid, targetUid, legalSquares: [{row,col}], hover: {row,col} | null }`. (AC387, AC388)
- **R26.A10 No hover flicker.** Find and fix the cause of hand cards (and close-up) flickering or going see-through on hover; write the cause in REPORT.md. Sweeping slowly across the hand keeps every hand card and its face at opacity ≥ 0.99, a card's classes change at most a few times as it is entered/left, and nothing changes while the pointer holds still. (AC389)
- **R26.B11 OH_ clips by name.** For every creature, `creature3d.ts` uses the clip names in `models.json` `clips` (and `events.json`); never "the clip at the same position". A role missing from the map uses the old Meshy clip of that role by name and is listed in REPORT.md as "missing clip". `getRenderStats().figures[i].currentClip` = name of the clip the figure is playing now. (AC390)
- **R26.B12 Parts like the Model Editor.** Copy every accessory's `partsTableLook` (bone, pos, rot in degrees XYZ, scale) from `factory-design/design/models/editor-setups-2026-10-28.json` into `PARTS`, and copy the listed part GLBs from `factory-design/design/models/parts/` into `public/models/creatures/<id>/` (Shiv Rat backpack + new dagger, Gnome Tinker scepter/crystal shield/wrench, Gyronaut wings and its new `char.glb`). Heights stay as now. `figures[i].parts = [{ name, file, bone, pos: [x,y,z], rotDeg: [x,y,z], scale: [x,y,z], loaded }]` (bone-local values as applied). (AC392, AC393)
- **R26.B13/B14 Gyronaut and Aegis Tinkerer** get their gear from the setups file, plus `events.json` and a `clips` map in `models.json` whose clip names all exist in their `char.glb` (missing OH_ clips listed in REPORT.md). Do not create or re-encode geometry; no Meshy. (AC394)
- **R26.C Failing checks.** Fix the game, not the checks: phone/odd-window HUD and hand positions (AC24, AC212, AC188, AC138), Tutorial Skip (AC25), Mine Control stance (AC341), Card Comp watch bar Pause (AC368/AC344, keep bottom-right at 0.58), Bowler Brawler bowling (AC360/AC361). (AC395)

### Round 24 (Skullflinger's skull throw) — DISCONTINUED: removed by Round 26 item R26.0; history only, do not build

Keep everything from Rounds 8a–23 and the owner's direct changes. **Do not touch any 3D model file.** `public/models/creatures/goblin-slinger/char.glb` (now with a 6th clip, `Skull_Throw`, 1.6 s) and `events.json` (event `projectile_release` at 0.6333 s from bone `RightHand`) are owner files: never re-export, re-compress or edit them. Keep `dist/` under 95 MB (AC90).

- **R24.1 The attack clip.** When Skullflinger attacks, his figure plays `Skull_Throw` once (not looping), then goes back to Idle. The clip name and release time are read from `events.json` (not hard-coded). The skull mace in his right hand stays visible the whole time. (AC369)
- **R24.2 The projectile.** At the release time, a flying skull spawns at the world position of his `RightHand` bone. It uses the skull from `skull-mace.glb` (loaded once and shared by every throw, about the same size as the one in his hand). The game moves it, not the animation. It flies to the target's chest height in a short arc (peak ~0.6 squares above the straight line for a far target, less for a close one) and spins end over end about 2 turns per second. It stays in the air ~0.35–0.55 s. If the target is gone before the skull lands, it lands where the target was and bounces once. (AC369)
- **R24.3 The bonk.** On contact the skull bounces off: it pops up and back the way it came (a little to one side), spins faster, hops once on the ground and fades out within ~0.7 s. At contact there is a quick white star/puff, 3–5 bone chips, a short white flash and a tiny squash/shake on the target, and the target plays its `Hit_Reaction` (`hit`). A short "bonk" uses an existing hit sound if one fits (no new sound files). The damage number may appear when the skull lands, but damage, timing, range and every rule number stay exactly as now. (AC370)
- **R24.4 General and light.** The throw reuses or extends the shared ranged/projectile code, so any unit can later use `events.json` plus a projectile model the same way. There are only a few projectiles on screen at once, and every projectile and effect is cleaned up when done.
- **R24.5 Test hooks (test mode).** `getRenderStats().creatureAnimLog` entries add `clip` (the GLB clip actually played, e.g. `{ uid, cardId: "goblin-slinger", anim: "attack", clip: "Skull_Throw" }`). Figure `items[i]` adds `visible` (boolean). `getRenderStats().projectiles` = projectiles on screen now: `[{ id, kind: "skull", shooterUid, targetUid, state: "flying"|"bouncing"|"fading", height }]` (`height` = squares above the ground). `getRenderStats().projectileLog` = every projectile this match, kept after removal: `[{ id, kind, shooterUid, targetUid, clip, bone, releaseMs, spawnOffset, contactMs, removedMs, heightAtContact, maxHeightAfterContact, hitTarget }]`. All times are game-clock ms since the attack clip started; `removedMs` is `null` while it is alive. `spawnOffset` = distance in squares from the spawn point to the `RightHand` bone at release. `hitTarget` = it reached a living target.
- **R24.6 Re-verify.** Run the whole suite. The last results file is from Round 22, so confirm that the Round 23 fixes (AC25, AC272, AC341, AC344, AC360, AC363–AC368) pass, and fix the remaining real failures: AC58 (ready-Miner counts on mine taps), AC168 (a weight-100 font face), AC176 (deck-pile picture), AC89, AC190, AC191, AC196, AC197 and AC261 (timeouts: make them faster, not longer).
- **R24.7 Report.** Under **"Editor updates needed"** in `REPORT.md`, list the projectile size, arc height, spin, bounce settings and effect names. Include the pass count and 1920 × 1080 screenshots of Skullflinger mid-throw and of the bonk.

### Round 23 (the hand like the layout editor, failing checks, flying units, Gnome/Dwarf model sizes) — built

Source: "Round 23" and the "Owner rule for every round (Oct 25)" at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md` (binding). Keep everything from 8a–22.

- **Owner's direct changes since Round 22 — do NOT change or undo:** Gnome and Dwarf 3D models for 29 units (`public/models/creatures/<id>/`, character only), the 16:9 letterbox in matches on computers (no rotate message on a computer), crystal colors, status icons above heads, the ability icon in the stat badge, the Card Comp vs Card Comp controls (watch bar moved bottom-right, scaled 0.58), and `.vercelignore`. Do not touch any 3D model file. Built `dist/` stays under **95 MB** (AC90). The owner already updated AC24, AC24b, AC80, AC84, AC355 and the Gnome/Dwarf AC357 check; they are binding.
- **R23.1 Hand fan = the owner's layout editor (top priority).** The in-match hand uses the `hand` piece of `src/content/owner-layout.json`: `overlap` 0.2, `angle` 4, `arc` 1.45, `slide` 18, `straighten`, `popScale` 1.6, `popY` 10, `ease` smooth, `speed` 450 — read from the file, not hard-coded. Remove the `cw * 0.62` spacing in `app.ts` `layoutHand`. Same formula as the editor preview (`factory-design/owner-tools/game-editor-source.html`, hand preview): with card width `w`, card *i* of *n* (k = i − (n−1)/2) sits `overlap × w` to the right of card *i−1* (center to center), is rotated `k × angle` degrees and lowered `arc × k² %` of the card height; later cards sit on top. Hovering a card straightens it (0°) and enlarges it `popScale` (1.6×), raised by `popY`; with `full`, the raised hand comes up fully on screen. Only when the fan would not fit the hand box may spacing shrink below `overlap × w`. (AC363, AC272)
- **R23.2 Tuck after grab.** As soon as a hand card is picked up (pointer down + move past the drag threshold), every other hand card slides back to the tucked rest position, and stays tucked after the card is dropped, played or cancelled (until the pointer hovers the hand again). (AC364)
- **R23.3 Auto-tuck.** When the pointer leaves the hand fan — onto the board, anywhere, or onto the part of the enlarged card that sticks out above the fan — the hand starts tucking within about 1/3 s (`getHandHover().raised` false within 0.5 s) and is fully down within ~1.5 s. Nothing may stay raised in real play (test flag off). (AC365)
- **R23.4 Hooks + real-mouse tests.** All hand checks run with real mouse events (`page.mouse.move` in steps, `down`, `up`) after setting `window.__app.test = false` (test-mode hooks keep working). `window.oreheart.getHandHover()` keeps its fields (`raised`, `raisedIndex`, `zone`, `cards`, …) and adds **`fan`**: one entry per hand card in hand order, `{ cardId, cx, cy, width, height, rotateDeg, scale }` = the card's current on-screen center (window CSS px), its unscaled, unrotated size, its current rotation (degrees, clockwise positive) and current scale, as drawn right now (mid-animation included). (AC363–AC365)
- **R23.5 Real failures from Round 22 (fix the game, checks stay):**
  - Tutorial **"Skip Tutorial"** button must be clickable (nothing covering it, stable) and return to the main menu (AC25).
  - **Mine Control stance:** per the brief's stance rules (Round 17), a Miner next to an enemy Miner **attacks** it when it survives the strike back and crystals are not low (can afford most of the hand); it mines only if it would die, or when crystals are low. The game is wrong (it mined with 16 crystals and Shield 1); fix the AI, the check stays (AC341).
  - **Card Comp vs Card Comp watch bar** (bottom-right, owner's 0.58 scale kept): **Play** starts and the button then reads **Pause**; Pause stops the match (no turn passes) and reads **Play** again; **Exit to menu** (accessible name) returns to the main menu; the match plays to the end at 4× (AC344, AC368).
  - **Bowler Brawler bowling** check (AC360) must finish well inside its 60 s limit (model load + two throws); fix any stall in the `bowl` sequence or ball state returning to `"hand"`.
- **R23.6 Flying units hover.** A unit whose saved look has `flying` (Model Editor → Flying, e.g. Thunderwing Gyronaut `gyrocopter-ace`) floats above its square: lift `hoverSquares`, bob ± `floatSquares` at `floatsPerSecond`, sway ± `swayDeg`, soft ground shadow if `groundShadow`. Without a saved look, `gyrocopter-ace` uses lift 0.35, bob 0.06, 0.6 per second, sway 3°, shadow on. Hooks on `getRenderStats().figures[i]`: `flying` = `{ hoverSquares, floatSquares, floatsPerSecond, swayDeg, groundShadow, source: "look"|"default" }` or `null` for walkers; `lift` = current height of the model's base above the ground (squares; 0 for walkers); `swayNowDeg` = current sway angle; `groundShadow` (boolean). (AC366)
- **R23.7 Gnome/Dwarf model sizes.** `creature3d.ts` `DEFAULT_H` (board squares): every Dwarf ~0.95, every Gnome ~0.85, Queen Brunhilda (`queen-brunhilda-stonebeard`) 1.05, Thunderhorn Ram Rider (`ram-rider`) 1.35, The Arcane Colossus (`the-great-steam-golem`) 1.6, Iron Scarab Automaton (`clockwork-beetle`) 0.9. An owner look's height still wins. No accessories. The Clockwork Beetle has no skeleton: moving slides it smoothly to the new square (logs `walk` in `creatureAnimLog`), being damaged shakes it (logs `hit`). (AC367)

### Phase 1B, Round 17 (three game modes: Standard, Card Play with stance buttons, Card Comp vs Card Comp; plus fixes) — built

Source: "Round 17" at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md` (binding). Keep everything from Rounds 8a–16. Commit + push after each numbered step. Rules, numbers and cards are unchanged.

**A. Fixes first (from Round 16's 57 failing checks)**
- **R17.1 One visible start button.** Choose Your Commander has exactly one start button, labelled **"Fight!"** (no hidden "Start" button anywhere; it replaces "Start" from R9c). Nothing covers it, so a normal click works. At 844×390 nothing on this screen overlaps or is cut off: Commander cards, deck stacks (names, **Remove …**, **Copy … to my decks**), **Easy / Normal / Hard**, the three mode buttons, **Back** and **Fight!** (AC338, AC346; every check that starts a match from this screen clicks "Fight!").
- **R17.2 The hand.** The hand container has `aria-label="Your hand"`; each hand card is a `<button>` inside it carrying `data-card-id`. **At rest the hand is an even fan fully on screen** (no card below the bottom edge; this replaces the Round 10 "quarter tuck" — the owner said it must not sit mostly below the edge). Hover still raises/enlarges the hovered card (straightened, neighbors aside, one at a time, nothing sticks); while dragging a card over the board the rest of the hand still tucks down (R11.9). On a phone a raised card covers less than half of the screen (AC43, AC163, AC174, AC175, AC251, AC347).
- **R17.3** `window.oreheart` is `undefined` without `?test=1` (AC5). Crystal mines show their owner color; unowned mines are white (owner, Oct 24, F.2).
- **R17.4** Then the rest of the failing list in `test-results.md` (Ranged, Brewlord, mine tapping, facing after a move, shard count, font weights, Deck Builder layout). Fix the game, not the checks.

**B. Three game modes**
- **R17.5 Choosing the mode.** On Choose Your Commander, next to Easy / Normal / Hard, three buttons with exactly these names: **"Standard"**, **"Card Play"**, **"Card Comp vs Card Comp"** (`aria-pressed="true"` on the chosen one; default Standard). The choice is remembered (localStorage) like the Commander, deck and difficulty. Easy/Normal/Hard still sets the enemy's skill. `getState().mode` is `"standard"`, `"cardplay"` or `"compvscomp"`.
  - **Standard** is today's game, exactly unchanged; no stance buttons are shown.
- **R17.6 Card Play.** Same board, cards, rules and computer opponent as Standard. The player only plays cards (summon, spells, Tunnel, Crystal cards, ability buttons on your turn). The player can never move, attack, mine or swap with units: in Card Play `legalActions()` for the player contains no `move`, `attack`, `mine` or `swap`, `act()` rejects them (`ok: false`, state unchanged), and dragging a unit or tapping a mine does nothing. In the player's Action phase the player's units act on their own, one by one, in **reading order (lowest row first, then lowest column)**, with their normal move/attack/mine animations, following the current stance and every rule (move ranges, summoning sickness, mining is an action, Ranged lines, abilities, strike back). A small speed toggle **"1×" / "2×"** and **Done** (skip the rest of your units' actions) are shown. The enemy plays as now.
- **R17.7 Stance buttons.** A container `aria-label="Stance"` on the right edge of the match screen, vertically centered (its middle within 8 % of the screen height from the center), holding 4 big buttons in this order, each with an icon and a short label: **"Aggressive"** (sword), **"Mine Control"** (pickaxe), **"Guard"** (shield), **"Free-for-all"** (crossed swords). About 72 px tall at 1920×1080 (at least 60 px) and at least 40 px on an 844×390 phone. The active one has `aria-pressed="true"` and glows in the player's faction color. Each has a `title` (one-line description, shown on hover / long-press). Switch any time, also mid-Action; the next unit to act uses the new stance. Default at match start: **Free-for-all**. Shown in Card Play and Card Comp vs Card Comp only. Stance AI (each unit, when it acts):
  - **Aggressive**: Miners go to the mines and mine. Fighters and Ranged go straight for the enemy Commander: if they can attack the enemy Commander they do; otherwise they move to reduce their distance (Chebyshev) to the enemy stronghold; they attack other units only when those block the path.
  - **Mine Control**: Miners mine. A Miner next to an enemy Miner attacks it only if it survives the strike back (Shield, Health, type advantage all counted, use `previewAttack`), and only if crystals are not low; otherwise it mines. **Low on crystals** = the hand is not empty and Flow + Bank can pay for fewer than half of the hand's cards. Fighters and Ranged hunt enemy Miners and enemy units next to mines first; once the player controls every mine or the enemy has no Miners, they attack the enemy Commander.
  - **Guard**: Fighters stay within 2 spaces (Chebyshev) of the player's stronghold (7,2) or one of the player's Miners; they attack enemies that come within reach and never chase beyond that. Miners keep mining but step away from a fight they would lose.
  - **Free-for-all**: the same full AI as the computer opponent (current difficulty).
- **R17.7b Card Comp vs Card Comp.** Both sides are played completely by the computer (mulligan, cards, units, abilities) with the full AI; the player's side uses the chosen deck and Commander. No timer waits for input; **Done** is hidden. A watch bar shows **"Pause"** (becomes **"Play"** while paused), speed **"1×" / "2×" / "4×"**, and **"Exit to menu"** (asks nothing, returns to the main menu). The stance buttons are shown and steer the player side's AI (Free-for-all by default). At the end the normal match-end screen shows who won plus a **"Rematch"** button (same choices, same mode).
- **R17.8 Test hooks** (test mode only, in addition to section 4):
  - `setMode('standard'|'cardplay'|'compvscomp')` → sets the remembered mode (same as clicking the mode button) for the next match.
  - `setStance('aggressive'|'minecontrol'|'guard'|'freeforall')`, `getStance()` → the current stance name; the stance buttons update at once.
  - `stanceChoice(creatureUid)` → the action object the current stance would choose for that player unit right now (an action as accepted by the engine, e.g. `{ type: 'attack', creatureUid, target: { commander: 'opponent' } }`, `{ type: 'move', creatureUid, row, col }`, `{ type: 'mine', creatureUid, row, col }`, or `{ type: 'wait', creatureUid }`), without applying it. Pure and seeded.
  - `autoActStep()` → makes the next player unit (reading order, among those that can still act) perform its stance action; returns `{ ok: true, action }`, or `{ ok: true, done: true }` when no unit can act. Works in the player's Action phase in Card Play and Card Comp vs Card Comp.
  - `newMatch` options: `mode` (default: `"standard"` in test mode, regardless of the remembered mode), `stance` (default `"freeforall"`), `unitAutoPlay` (Card Play only; default `true` = in test mode units act immediately when the player's Action phase begins; `false` = they act only through `autoActStep()` or never if Done is pressed), `paused` (Card Comp vs Card Comp only: start paused until "Play" is pressed). In test mode Card Comp vs Card Comp runs without animation waits once playing. `setup` works with every mode.

Report: screenshots 1920×1080 and 844×390 of the fight menu with the three modes, a Card Play match with the stance buttons, a Card Comp vs Card Comp match with the watch bar, and a short sequence of units acting on their own under Aggressive and Mine Control; plus the pass count.

### Phase 1B, Round 15 (Round 14 verified, facing, Bowler Brawler's bowling ball, all text white, unit stat badges) — READ FIRST

Source: "Round 15" at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md` (binding). Keep everything from Rounds 8a–14. Commit + push after each numbered step.

**R15.0 Make Round 14 work first.** Every Round 14 item (R14.1–R14.5 below) must pass its checks. **Test hooks:** `/?test=1` (with or without `&seed=N`) MUST turn test mode on and define `window.oreheart` with every hook in section 4 and Rounds 12c–14 (`newMatch`, `getState`, `legalActions`, `act`, `getCard`, `listCards`, `squareToScreen`, `getFlowStreams`, `flowShardCount`, `getAbilityStatus`, `cardFormat`, …) before the first frame finishes loading; without `test=1` it stays `undefined` (AC5). Round 14 gated the hooks in a way the checks could not reach — this is the main cause of the ~100 failures. Do not require any other flag, storage value or click to enable test mode.

**R15.1 Facing.** Every creature on the board stores a facing, one of `"N" | "NE" | "E" | "SE" | "S" | "SW" | "W" | "NW"`, where **N = toward row 1** (the enemy's back row) and **E = toward column 13**. It is exposed as `facing` on each creature in `getState().board.creatures` and as `data-facing` on the creature's labelled element (the one named "<Name>, row R, column C"). Rules:
- A newly placed creature faces the enemy side: player units `N`, opponent units `S`.
- After a move, it faces the direction of the **last step** of its path (a straight move from (6,6) to (6,8) → `E`; (6,8) to (4,8) → `N`).
- After an attack (melee or Ranged) or an ability aimed at a square/creature, it faces the target (sign of the row and column difference; e.g. target directly right → `E`).
- After mining, it faces the mine it mined (miner at (5,3), mine at (4,2) → `NW`) and keeps it.
- Facing never resets: not at end of turn, not at the start of its owner's turn, not after a re-render, swap, knock-back or save/load. Being pushed (Strike, Plow, knock-back) does not change facing.
- The 3D figure turns to that facing smoothly (≈ 150–250 ms) and holds it; nothing snaps back to face the player's side. Commanders keep their own facing rules (AC230/AC281).
- The AI's units follow the same rules.

**R15.2 Bowler Brawler's bowling ball (Strike).** `goblin-scrapper`, ability `strike`, cost 2 (Flow first, then Bank), once per turn, Action phase. Called as `act({ type: 'ability', creatureUid, ability: 'strike', target: { row, col } })` where `target` is one of the 4 orthogonally adjacent squares; it chooses the direction (front, back, left or right). The UI is the Strike ability button then a click on an adjacent square (AC320).
- The ball starts next to the Bowler and rolls square by square in that direction until it leaves the board or reaches a crystal mine or a Stronghold square (those stop it). It passes through creatures: **every creature on its path (friend or foe, not Commanders) is knocked sideways**, at a right angle to the path, never backwards or forwards.
- Distance: 1 or 2 squares (seeded RNG). Side: random (seeded RNG). If the first square on the chosen side is blocked (off the board, a mine, a Stronghold or any unit), it goes to the other side instead. If it rolled 2 but the second square is blocked, it stops after 1. If both sides are blocked at the first square, it stays put.
- Creatures are processed in the order the ball reaches them; a creature already knocked aside is no longer on the path. Knocking deals no damage. The Bowler's facing turns toward the roll (R15.1).
- Visual: a big stone/wood/rusty-metal bowling ball (like his card art) rolls visibly, spinning, with a little dust, from the Bowler to where it stops/leaves; each hit creature tumbles sideways like a pin. The AI uses the same rules and may use Strike.
- Card text (put in `cards.json` `text`, `shortText`, `longText`; `longText` adds an "Example:" line): "Nearby Goblins get +1 Attack. Strike (2 crystals): Roll a huge bowling ball in a straight line (front, back, left or right). Like bowling pins, every creature in its path, friend or foe, is knocked 1-2 spaces off to the side. If that side is blocked, it goes to the other side."

**R15.3 All text white — except on cards.** Every visible text outside card faces has computed color **`rgb(255, 255, 255)`** (#ffffff): title gate, main menu, Rules/How to Play, Settings and all its panels, Deck Builder (title, filters, labels, deck list, buttons), Choose Your Commander, the match HUD (names, Flow/Bank numbers, turn banner, phase list, timer, Menu and Done, tooltips, ability button labels, badges, hearts), pop-ups and dialogs. This **overrides** every earlier gold/#FFD700/brown-gold text rule and the text colors in the owner layout and faction themes (positions, sizes, weights and fonts from the layout still apply). Keep shadows/outlines and hover glows; letters stay white in every state. `--text-white` stays `#ffffff`. Card faces (anything inside an element with `data-card-id` or `data-card-back`) are unchanged.

**R15.4 Unit stat badge (owner design, binding).** Copy `factory-design/unit-badges/*.webp` and `badges.json` into `public/art/ui/badges/` and follow `badges.json`.
- Every creature on the board (not Commanders/Strongholds — they keep their heart) has, inside its labelled element, one badge element `[data-unit-badge]` with `data-metal` = `rust` (Goblins) / `silver` (Gnomes) / `gold` (Dwarves) and `data-type` = `pickaxe` (role Gatherer/Miner or Smith Miner), `bow` (any card whose `range` is not null), otherwise `sword`.
- It contains an `<img>` whose `src` ends with `badges/frame-<metal>.webp` and an `<img>` whose `src` ends with `badges/icon-<type>-<metal>.webp`, the icon placed over the center circle (`badges.json` placeAt).
- Three live text numbers, in DOM order Attack, Health, Movement: `[data-stat="attack"]`, `[data-stat="health"]`, `[data-stat="movement"]`, showing current values (damage, healing, buffs update them; keep the red/green change flash and the red current-Health rule). Font **Russo One** (bundled), white, thick black outline (`-webkit-text-stroke` or text-shadow) and soft shadow, positions/sizes from `badges.json`.
- The badge sits under the unit's feet (its center is below the square's screen center from `squareToScreen`, horizontally centered on it), faces the camera, stays readable at every zoom and never covers the unit. It replaces the old stat icons/pill. The creature label's text still reads Attack … Health … Movement (AC30).

Report: 1920×1080 screenshot sequences listed in the brief (unit moving and keeping facing, miner facing a node, the bowling ball scattering two creatures, one with a blocked side; title, a menu and the Deck Builder in white; a close-up of one unit per faction with its badge) and the checks pass count.

Source: "Round 14" at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md` (binding) and the owner's Oct 24 layout `src/content/owner-layout.json` (byte-identical to `factory-design/owner-layouts/final-draft/layout.json`, 34 841 bytes). - The served copy `/owner-layouts/final-draft/layout.json` (in `public/`) must be the same bytes as `src/content/owner-layout.json` (34 841 bytes; today `public/` still holds the old 34 481-byte file). One source, no hand-copied numbers.

For text pieces (`text` object, including the Menu and Done buttons): `color`, `size` (in `cqh`, i.e. percent of the frame height × `textScale`), `font`, `bold` (computed weight ≥ 600), `italic`, `align`, `valign`, `shadow`, `glow`/`glowColor`, `caps` exactly. The computed `color` of the element that draws the text must equal the layout hex (all current HUD text is `#ffffff`); font size within 10 %.
- Changed this time: `pName` (your Commander name, e.g. "Grubnak Rotgut") now sits at y 70.99 next to the Flow crystal; Menu text size 3.7 at its new position; Done text size 5.7; the Flow crystal `pCrystal` moved slightly.

**R14.2 Fanned hands.**
- Your hand follows the layout's `hand` settings: card `k` places from the centre is rotated `k × angle` (angle 4°) and dropped by `arc × k²` (arc 1.45, % units as in the Game Editor), neighbours overlap by `overlap` 0.2, cards sit in the `hand` box (h 33.81 % of the frame, still tucked at rest as before). Hovering a card straightens it (0°) and pops it up `popScale` 1.6× and `popY` 10 % (slide), neighbours move aside. Each hand card button carries `data-fan-angle="<degrees currently drawn>"` (e.g. −8, −4, 0, 4, 8 for five cards; 0 while popped). Drag-to-play, reorder and the tuck/raise behaviour from Rounds 11–12a stay.
- The enemy hand (`eHand`) is a fan of card backs: one back per enemy card, at most `fan.count` 6, consecutive backs `fan.angle` 9° apart, centred on 0, the whole fan rotated by the piece's `rot` (−180). Each back carries `data-fan-angle`.

**R14.3 Crystal flow to the Flow crystal icon.** Shards from a side's mines fly to that side's Flow crystal HUD piece (`player-flow-crystal` bottom-left, `enemy-flow-crystal` top-right), not to the Commander or stronghold. `getFlowStreams()` returns `{ log: [{ side, from, to, toPoint: { x, y } (CSS px, inside the target crystal's box), count, flow, shards, sBends }], pulses: { player, opponent } }`; when shards arrive the crystal plays a short pulse and its `pulses` counter goes up.

**R14.4 More shards with more Flow.** Shards per mine per stream = `window.oreheart.flowShardCount(flow)` where `flow` is that side's Flow for the turn: 2 at Flow 1, never decreasing, about 12 (10–14) at Flow 16 (suggested `min(12, 1 + ceil(flow × 0.7))`, any monotone curve meeting those points is fine). Each log entry's `count` equals `flowShardCount(entry.flow)`.

**R14.5 Owner card changes.**
- **Goblin Rocket Rider** (`shard-guzzler`) is now a **Fighter** (`role` `fighter` in `getCard`): mines 1 like other Fighters, no Miner ⛏️ badge, summons like a Fighter. Its rocket ability is unchanged.
- **Corpsman Brewlord** (`brewmaster`) — owner text: "Once per turn, at any time (even on the enemy's turn): heal an adjacent wounded dwarf. If that dwarf would die, it survives with 1 Health instead. It becomes Drunk for 2 rounds. Drunk dwarves can't be healed." Engine ability name `brew`, cost 0, target = an adjacent (8 neighbours) wounded friendly Dwarf-faction creature that is not Drunk (not itself, not a Commander).
  - Usable once per turn (resets at the start of each player's turn), in any phase of either player's turn, as `act({ type: 'ability', creatureUid, ability: 'brew', target })`. The ability button (`data-ability="brew"`, icon `mug`) is lit on the enemy's turn too.
  - Effect: heal the target to full; it becomes Drunk with `drunk: 2` (state field on the creature; `newMatch` setup board entries accept `drunk`). Drunk counts down by 1 at the start of its owner's turn, so it lasts 2 rounds.
  - Save: when an enemy attack, spell or ability would defeat a non-Drunk friendly dwarf adjacent to a Brewlord whose `brew` is unused this turn, the damage pauses and a Respond window opens for the Brewlord's owner (`pendingResponse.responder` = that side; `legalActions()` includes the `brew` ability action). Using it: the dwarf survives with exactly 1 Health and becomes Drunk (2). "Let it happen" lets it die. The AI uses `brew` to heal and to save.
  - Drunk creatures can't be healed by anything: healing spells (Field Bandage, …), abilities and the start-of-turn refresh to full all skip them. Their token shows a 🍺 Drunk icon and carries `data-drunk="<rounds left>"` (absent or `0` when sober); hover/tap text says "Drunk: can't be healed (N rounds)".

Not in this round: new models, Meshy, other layout changes, the queued Menu Builder/halls work.

### Phase 1B, Round 12d (card faces use the owner's card format) — done

Source: "Round 12d" at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md` on `main` (binding) and `factory-design/card-format/README.md` (read it first). Keep everything from Rounds 8a–12c. **No new abilities, no rule changes, no 3D work, no Meshy.** Keep it small and **commit + push after each step** (1 port, 2 use everywhere, 3 compare with the references). If time runs short, stop after the last finished step and report what is done.

Owner files (binding, use unchanged):
- `projects/oreheart-8/public/art/card-format/format.json` (name `"Card format final 1.0"`; layouts `unit`, `commander`, `spell`, `instant`, `crystal`, `back`; `frames`, `images`, `builtin`, `factions`) and `public/art/card-format/images/` (frames, gems, cost badges, emblems).
- On `main`: `factory-design/card-format/render.js` (the owner's Art Studio renderer: `makeOHRenderer(format)` → `{ cardHTML(card, artUrl, heightPx), fitText(el), groupOf(card) }`), `card.css`, and `reference/*.jpg` (goblin-scrounger, gnome-prospector, rune-splitter, fireball, healing-light, soul-shard, venom-shiv, second-wind).

**R12d.1 Port the renderer.** Copy `render.js` and `card.css` into the game (a TypeScript wrapper around the same code is fine; do not re-invent the layout). At start-up the game loads `/art/card-format/format.json` once and builds the renderer from it. One layout per card type, chosen exactly as `groupOf`: creatures → `unit`, Commanders → `commander`, regular spells → `spell`, Instants → `instant`, Crystal cards → `crystal`, card backs → `back`. Faction looks come from the per-faction overrides in format.json (`fg`, `byFaction`, `frames`). The art layer honors the card's `artPos` `{s,x,y}` exactly as render.js does (translate x%, y%, scale s, clipped inside an `artclip` wrapper); Crystal cards use the format's full-bleed art. After a face is inserted into the page, `fitText` runs on it so overflowing text shrinks to fit (never clipped, never truncated with "…"). Fonts **Alegreya SC**, **Russo One** and **IM Fell English SC** are bundled as local font files in the app (self-hosted `@font-face`, no Google Fonts request for them) and listed in `CREDITS.md`.

**Card face markup contract (tests rely on it).** Every card face element in the game (the element that carries `data-card-id="<card id>"`, as before) contains exactly one renderer root `div.cardc` (the class render.js emits) and carries two new attributes: `data-card-format="Card format final 1.0"` (the format's `name`) and `data-layout="unit|commander|spell|instant|crystal|back"`. The renderer root keeps the 420 : 624 proportions (never stretched). Card backs (enemy hand, deck piles that show a card back, mulligan backs if any) are elements with `data-layout="back"` containing a `div.cardc`, drawn from the `back` layout. Commander cards on Choose Your Commander and the Deck Builder "Commander card" tile are faces with `data-card-id="<commander id>"` and `data-layout="commander"`. The existing accessible labels stay on each face (`aria-label`s such as `Cost N`, `Attack N`, `Health N`, `Movement N`, `Type: …`, and `data-frame` where older checks use it); where a layout has no visible layer for one of them, the label sits on a visually hidden element inside the face. Card text inside `.cardc` uses the format's own fonts (the one-font rule R5a.1 applies to everything outside card faces).

**New test hooks** on `window.oreheart` (test mode):
- `cardFormat()` → `{ name, layouts: string[] }` from the loaded format.json (e.g. `{ name: "Card format final 1.0", layouts: ["unit","spell","crystal","commander","back","instant"] }`).
- `renderCard(cardId, heightPx)` → Promise of the HTML string the ported renderer produces for that card (or Commander id) at that height, with the same art picture the game uses (`pictureOf` / `OWNER_ART` / Commander portrait). `renderCard("back", h)` renders the card back. `fitText(el)` runs the ported `fitText` on an element (so a test can insert `renderCard` HTML into the page and fit it). The game's `card.css` is loaded globally, so inserted HTML lays out exactly like in-game faces. The returned HTML's root is `<div class="cardc" style="width:Wpx;height:Hpx">` with W = H × 420 / 624.

**R12d.2 Use it everywhere.** Hand cards, the hover pop-out, the dragged card, the card close-up (match and Deck Builder), the Deck Builder grid and deck pile, played-card pop-ups (`Card reveal`), the mulligan cards, How to Play / menu cards, Choose Your Commander cards, the Commander card tile and every card back are drawn by the new renderer. **Delete the old frame code and the old frame images** (`public/art/frames/` and any other old card-frame/spell-frame/rarity-trim images outside `public/art/card-format/`), so nothing can fall back to them: the folder `public/art/frames` no longer exists and the game never requests any URL containing `/art/frames/`.

**R12d.3 Check against the references.** Render the 8 reference cards at 420 × 624 and compare with `factory-design/card-format/reference/*.jpg` (same frame, gem, cost badge, name/type/text positions and fonts); fix differences. (The reference renders used fallback fonts, so lettering may differ slightly; frames, layout, gems, icons and positions must match.) Report side-by-sides of 3 cards next to their references plus 1920 × 1080 screenshots of a hand, a card close-up, the Deck Builder and Choose Your Commander.

### Phase 1B, Round 12c (make every card do what its text says) — done

Source: "Round 12c" at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md` (binding). Keep everything from Rounds 8a–12b-1. **The owner's `cards.json` (names, numbers, rarities, roles, `text`, `artPos`) is now the source of truth and must not be edited by the builder.** No 3D models, no Meshy. Work in this order and **commit + push after each step**: 1 display fixes, 2 numbers and ranges, 3 Goblins, 4 Gnomes, 5 Dwarves, 6 neutral spells and Crystal cards. If time runs short, stop after the last finished step and report which cards are done.

**R12c.0 Card text, older rules and older checks.**
- A card face (hand, close-up, Deck Builder, menus, pop-ups) shows the card's owner `text` in full, never truncated (the 80-character `shortText` limit no longer applies to owner text; `getCard().text` = the owner text; `shortText` may equal it). `longText` keeps its existing format (plain explanation, ends with a line starting "Example:") and must describe exactly what the engine does. How to Play/Rules wording matches.
- Where a card's text conflicts with an older rule in this spec, **the card text wins for that card** (e.g. Scrapsmith Miner now draws a card when it dies, unless it died upgrading; Riot Shield Bearer is a Fighter, not a Miner).
- **Dwarves no longer have a basic Miner.** Dwarf starter decks keep 50 Miners as **50 Anvilsworn Miners** (Smith Miners); Goblin/Gnome decks keep 30 basic + 20 Smith. The free Miner (R6.2) is unchanged. Rename: the Goblin basic Miner is now **Goblin Miner** (same id `goblin-scrounger`).
- Older checks that hard-coded card names, costs, stats, roles or text the owner has now changed have been updated by the planner (AC6, AC10, AC18, AC27, AC45, AC51, AC112, AC247). If any other older check fails **only** because of the owner's new card data (stats/names/roles in its setup, e.g. using `dwarf-miner` as a 1/1 Miner), the builder may change that check's setup to an equivalent card, keeping its intent, and must list each such change in the report.
- New test hooks: `getCard(id)` and `listCards()` also return `artPos` `{s,x,y}` and `range` (Ranged reach in squares; `99` = any distance; `null` for non-Ranged). Creatures in `getState().board.creatures` gain `poison` (remaining Venom Shiv ticks, 0 if none), `ward` (boolean), `illusion` (boolean). `getAbilityStatus()` → `{ [cardId]: "done" | "not done" }` for every card listed in R12c.3–6 and the Ranged/spell cards in R12c.2, honestly reporting what is built (AC309).
- New actions: `{ type: "ability", creatureUid, ability, target?, to?, heals? }` with the ability names below; `{ type: "cast", uid, target?, to? }` (`to` only for Rat-Tunnel Ambush). Directions are given as `target` = the adjacent square in that direction. Every paid ability is paid like a spell (Flow first, then Bank), is listed in `legalActions()` only when affordable and legal, and is the creature's action for the turn unless stated. The AI considers every new ability and spell.

**R12c.1 Display fixes.** `artPos` framing on every card face: the picture is scaled by `s` and shifted by `x`%, `y`% of the art window, clipped to it (default `{s:1,x:0,y:0}`); same on Commander cards from `commanders.json`. Crystal cards show their picture full size like spells (frame overlays on top). Every `OWNER_ART` card uses its picture everywhere (Deck Builder grid and close-ups included); Borgo's portrait is `/art/owner/dwarf/borgo-portrait.webp`. **Betty Fizzlebottom (`sprocket`) is archived:** removed from Choose Your Commander, AI picks, starter decks (9 remain: 3 per faction) and tests; her data moves to `data/archived-cards.json`.

**R12c.2 Numbers and ranges.** Ranged attacks keep the R11.17 line rule (8 straight directions, shot stops at the first unit) with a **per-card range**: Skullflinger 6, Stormcaller Savant 5, Thunderwing Gyronaut 2 (Flying: moves over creatures), Marksmen Gunner 7, Grudge Hurler 5, Hexslinger Apprentice any distance (`range` 99, blocked by the first unit in the line if it is an ally; an enemy there is the target), every other Ranged card 7. Starfall Cataclysm: 4 damage to every creature in the 3×3 area centered on the target square. Venom Shiv: the target enemy creature gets `poison: 7`; at the start of each of its owner's turns it does not heal and takes 1 damage (`poison` −1); no healing of any kind while poisoned.

**R12c.3 Goblins.**
- SewerSlide Bomber `run` (pay 1, target = direction): moves square by square in that straight/diagonal line until the next square holds a unit, a mine or the board edge, then explodes: Ka-boom (1 damage, + upgrades' Attack bonus, to each adjacent enemy) and it dies. Normal Ka-boom when defeated too.
- Bare Knuckle Lunatic: when defeated, every unit (creature) adjacent to it is pushed 1 square directly away from it (blocked = stays).
- Bowler Brawler: aura +1 Attack to adjacent Goblins; `strike` (pay 2, target = orthogonal direction): every creature in that line (to the edge), friend or foe, is pushed 1–2 squares (seeded random each) further along the line; farthest first; blocked = stops early. The Brawler doesn't move.
- Backstabbing Thief: Sneaky (no strike back); each enemy creature it defeats moves 1 crystal from the opponent's Flow (else Bank) to the player's Flow; nothing if the opponent has none.
- Junk Shaman: adjacent Goblins +1 Attack on your turn; `pickUp` (pay 2, target = any creature within 5 (Chebyshev) of the Shaman, `to` = an empty non-mine square within 5 of the Shaman).
- Goblin Chef: melee; after its attack the target (if alive) is pushed 2 squares directly away from the Chef.
- Goblin Rocket Rider: `rocketRide` (free, target = any empty non-mine square): it moves there and every other creature in the 3×3 around it, friend or foe, takes damage = its Attack; then it may still make one attack this turn. Role stays `gatherer`.
- Pickpocket! (counterspell): a creature being summoned returns to its owner's hand (cost not refunded). Rat-Tunnel Ambush: cast with `target` = your creature and `to` = an empty square adjacent to a mine; that creature may still attack this turn.

**R12c.4 Gnomes.** Gearward Sentinel: Guard (an enemy adjacent to it may attack only it); once per enemy turn, after an enemy creature attacks one of your creatures, the Sentinel teleports to an empty square adjacent to the attacked creature and deals its Attack to the attacker. Glyphshield Warden: Shield 1 at the start of your turn (doesn't stack above 1); `ward` (pay 2, once per turn, target = ally): `ward: true` until the start of your next turn — it takes no damage and can't attack. Voldawright `shockwave` (pay 1): each adjacent enemy takes 1 damage and is pushed 1 square away. Mirrorveil Illusionist: `blink` costs 1 (once per match, swap with any of your creatures); when summoned it also places an illusion of itself (same stats, `illusion: true`, takes 35% less damage rounded down, can't attack, doesn't count as a card) on the empty square in front of it, if any. Brawler Adept: no ability (text only). Gnome Plow `plow` (pay 2, target = a square in a straight orthogonal line): it moves there; each creature in its path is pushed sideways 1–2 squares (seeded random side and distance). Overclocked Aethervein: mines 3; when you play a second or later Crystal card in one turn, it becomes unable to act this turn and takes 1 damage. Soul Stone Siphoner: +1 Bank for each creature it defeats; `sacrifice` (free, target = adjacent ally): that ally dies, +1 Bank. Unweave: counter any enemy spell. Snuff Out: counter a spell costing 3 or less (no Respond window opens if the only response can't legally cancel it).

**R12c.5 Dwarves.** Riot Shield Bearer (Fighter): for its owner's first 3 turns after it is summoned (summon turn counts) it can't move or attack and its Health can't drop below 1; setup-placed creatures start with this expired. Ironwall Shieldbeard: Guard; `corpsman` (free, once per round, `heals: [{row,col,amount}]`, adjacent allies, total ≤ 7). Grudge Hurler: Ranged 5 in lines; an enemy it damages can't move during its owner's next turn. Bloodbeard Berserker: +1 Attack permanently each time it survives being attacked, and +1 for every 2 enemies it defeats. Corpsman Brewlord: once per turn `medic` heals an adjacent wounded Dwarf to full. Thunderhorn Ram Rider: Charge; `gallop` (pay 2): move to any empty square in a straight or diagonal line, any distance, not through units. Queen Brunhilda: takes at most 3 damage from any single hit. Gemdrunk Deepdelver: Attack = printed + your current Bank (shown in state `attack`). Ironjaw Big Back: no ability. Granite Oathkeeper: when it attacks, every adjacent enemy is pushed 1–2 squares away (seeded). Oathbound Overwatch: Sneaky; may attack any enemy creature at any distance. Barrelback Courier: can move/attack the turn it's summoned. Door Splitter: Charge in any of 8 directions; the creature it hits is pushed 1–3 squares in a seeded random direction (friend or foe). Grimhold Ravager: Attack doubles this round if it attacked or was attacked during the previous round. Thunderhorn Blast: your creatures +1 Attack this turn. Ironclad Bulwark: the target's next incoming hit deals 0. Emergency Rations: heal your Commander (`{commander:"player"}`) or a creature 4 (not above max).

**R12c.6 Neutral spells and Crystal cards.** Field Bandage: heal one of your creatures to full (not if poisoned). Desperate Plunder (cost 0, no target): at the start of each of your turns, after your Flow is set, if the opponent's current Flow is greater than yours you gain +3 Flow (each copy adds 3); once you start a turn with more Flow than them it ends. Crystal cards are free and playable any time on your turn (R10.12 unchanged): Soul Shard +1 Bank, Soul Cluster +2 Bank, Heart of the Fallen +4 Bank, Motherlode +3 Bank and your next creature this match costs 2 less (minimum 0), Wellspring Geode +1 Flow at the start of each of your turns for the rest of the game.

**Report:** steps and cards done/not done, owner questions (see Assumptions), every older check changed for owner card data, screenshots at 1920×1080 of one new ability per faction and the Deck Builder with the new art.

### Phase 1B, Round 12b-1 (Goblin 3D models from the owner's sheets) — done

Source: the "Round 12b-1" and "Round 12" (items 3–7) sections at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md` (binding) and the GDD. Keep **everything** from Rounds 8a–12a; **rules, layout and HUD are unchanged**. **Build only the five models below**, in this order, and **commit + push after each model works**: 1 Grubgrabber Miner, 2 Boneknocker, 3 Shiv Rat, 4 Scrapsmith Miner, 5 Grubnak seated on his junk throne. If time runs short, stop after the last finished model and say in the report which are done; the checks for an undelivered model skip themselves (they key off `models.json`). NOT this round: Gnome and Dwarf models, Sproingle/Borgo thrones (Round 12b-2), any rule change.

**R12b.0 Meshy, once per model (build time only).** A build-time Node script `tools/gen-goblin-models-3d.mjs` (never run by the game; reads `MESHY_API_KEY` from the environment; current Meshy API, https://docs.meshy.ai) makes each model from the owner's sheet in `factory-design/goblin-art/` (binding: match its shapes, colors and gear exactly). The builder crops the sheet's views (front, side, back) into separate images and sends them as a **multi-image to 3D** job (front view first; image URLs `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Lummex/Starter/main/factory-design/goblin-art/<file>` or base64 data URIs of the crops), PBR textures, about 8–15k triangles per creature (≈ 30k for Grubnak, ≈ 10k for the throne), then **auto-rig** (humanoid) and add the closest library animations. Textures downscaled to ≤ 1024 (creatures) / ≤ 2048 (Grubnak, throne). Props on the sheet (pickaxe, club, dagger, hammer, backpack, lamp, staff) may be baked into the mesh or made as separate small Meshy/code-built props parented to the right bone; either way they must be visible and match the sheet. Each model is generated **once**; later builds must not call Meshy for it unless the owner asks; the running game requests no `meshy.ai` host. Each model replaces the old figure **on the board, in the 3D viewer (Deck Builder close-up) and, for Grubnak, on Choose Your Commander**. List every model in CREDITS.md (Meshy-generated from owner art). Report the Meshy credits spent. Total `dist/` stays under 40 MB (AC90): if needed, compress (meshopt/Draco is fine if the loader is set up) rather than drop a model.

**R12b.1–4 Goblin creature models.** Files go to `public/models/creatures/<cardId>.glb` (each ≤ 6 MB) and one manifest `public/models/creatures/models.json` = `{ "<cardId>": { "file": "<cardId>.glb", "sheet": "<owner sheet file name>", "taskIds": [..], "triangles": N, "bytes": N (real size), "parts": [..], "animations": { "<canonical>": "<Meshy clip name or 'procedural'>" } }, …, "creditsSpent": N }`, holding only the models actually delivered:

| # | Card (id) | Owner sheet | Required `parts` (canonical names) | Required animations |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Grubgrabber Miner (`goblin-scrounger`) | `goblin-miner-tpose-sheet.png`, assembled like `goblin-miner-assembled-reference.png` (`goblin-miner-wide-art.png` = look only) | `backpack` (crystal backpack worn on the back), `lantern` (on the backpack), `helmetLamp` (lit, glowing), `pickaxe` (held in both hands) | `idle`, `walk`, `attack` (pickaxe swing), `mine` |
| 2 | Boneknocker (`goblin-pebbler`) | `boneknocker-model-sheet.png` (hooded goblin) | `club` (spiked wooden club) | `idle`, `walk`, `attack` (the sheet's bottom-row swing poses) |
| 3 | Shiv Rat (`shiv-rat`) | `shiv-rat-model-sheet.png` (hooded goblin) | `dagger` (rusty) | `idle`, `walk`, `attack` (stab) |
| 4 | Scrapsmith Miner (`goblin-smith-miner`) | `apron-goblin-model-sheet-UNASSIGNED.png` | `apron`, `scarf` (red), `helmetLamp` (lit), `backpack`, `pickaxe`, `hammer` | `idle`, `walk` (sheet walk cycle), `attack` (smash), `mine` |

Animation names are canonical game names (map each to the clip used; a short procedural clip built in code from the sheet's poses is allowed when Meshy has no fitting clip, mapped as `"procedural"`). On the board: the figure stays about 1.1 spaces tall (0.9–1.3), stands on its square facing the opponent like other figures, keeps its ring, stat badges and bomb/other props, loops `idle`, plays `walk` while moving, `attack` when it attacks, `mine` when it mines (Miners), then returns to `idle`; poke reactions still play (reuse a clip). The helmet lamp(s) are visibly lit (emissive, small point light allowed on High graphics).
- **Test hook:** every `getRenderStats().figures[i]` gains `{ cardId, uid, row, col, source, height, parts, lampLit, animations, currentAnimation, animationLog }`: `source` = the GLB file name for a new model (e.g. `"goblin-scrounger.glb"`) or `"placeholder"`/current source for others; `parts` = canonical part names present and visible; `lampLit` = true when a lit helmet lamp is showing (false/absent otherwise); `animations` = canonical names available; `animationLog` = canonical one-shot/non-idle animations played since the match started, in order.
- **3D viewer:** the Deck Builder close-up of each card above shows its new model in the "3D model" region (R9c.6) with one plain-text button per animation (**Idle, Walk, Attack**, and **Mine** for the two Miners); `getViewerState().source` = `"<cardId>.glb"`; clicking a button sets `getViewerState().animation` to that canonical name.

**R12b.5 Grubnak seated on his junk throne.** New Grubnak model from `grubnak-model-sheet.png` (spiked crown with purple gems, fur-collar purple robe with the crowned-skull emblem, crystal-tipped staff; much bigger than a normal goblin) in a **seated** pose holding the staff, sitting on his **junk throne** from `grubnak-throne-model-sheet.png` (front/side/back views; static model). The throne **replaces the castle/stand** (`pFort`) in the Goblin stronghold spot whenever Grubnak is a Commander (player or enemy): same board point (player cx 1.5 / cy 6.5; enemy 11.5 / 0.5), flat on the ground, sized to the stronghold footprint, never moving. Grubnak sits on its seat at the same board point; Grubnak and throne both face the owner's `turn` (player 146°, enemy −15°; R9c.2 conventions; the old stronghold turn 137° no longer applies to the throne). Total height (throne + seated Grubnak) 2.2–2.6 spaces; Grubnak alone is clearly bigger than a goblin creature (seated height ≥ 1.3 spaces). He plays a seated idle holding the staff and keeps his reactions where they fit: all 8 canonical names (`idle`, `laugh`, `point`, `celebrate`, `surprised`, `cast`, `hit`, `defeat`) stay available (seated-friendly clips or procedural upper-body motion; R8b look-backs/impatience may reuse them) and still fire at the same moments (AC182); the rocket-barrel retreat on defeat may stay. The HUD heart, portrait and nothing-covers-the-Commander rules are unchanged.
- **Files:** the new Grubnak **replaces** `public/models/commanders/grubnak.glb` (≤ 10 MB); the throne is `public/models/commanders/grubnak-throne.glb` (≤ 5 MB). In `models.json` the `grubnak` entry is updated (new `taskIds`, `triangles`, `bytes`, `animations`, plus `"pose": "seated"` and `"sheet": "grubnak-model-sheet.png"`), a `throne` entry `{ file: "grubnak-throne.glb", sheet: "grubnak-throne-model-sheet.png", taskIds, triangles, bytes }` is added, and the old `fortress` and `scepter` entries and files are **removed** (an optional `staff` entry is allowed if the staff is a separate file). `creditsSpent` updated.
- **Test hooks:** `commanderModels` entry for a seated Grubnak: `source` `"grubnak.glb"`, `pose` `"seated"`, `throne` `"grubnak-throne.glb"`, `fortress` null, `height` = total height, `characterHeight` = Grubnak's own seated height in spaces, `held.item` `"staff"` (`held.bone` = a right-hand bone name, or `"baked"` if the staff is part of the mesh). `getPiecePoses().pFort` (or `eFort`) is the throne when that side's Commander is Grubnak; the Commander piece (`pCmd`/`eCmd`) gains `seated: true`, and its `baseY` is its lowest point **above** the board (on the seat). The Choose Your Commander model and the Deck Builder viewer show the seated Grubnak on his throne.
- **Screenshots (report):** at 1920 × 1080, each new model on the board and in the 3D viewer, and Grubnak on his throne (AC283 saves them).

### Phase 1B, Round 12a (drag-to-play fix, hand layout, owner Goblin card paintings) — done

Source: the "Round 12a" and "Round 12" sections at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md` (binding) and the GDD. Keep **everything** from Rounds 8a–11 unless an item below replaces it. **Build only these three items.** NOT this round: no new 3D models, no Meshy calls, no junk throne, no change to Grubnak's figure or stronghold (all Round 12b). Rules are unchanged. Keep the job short (target well under 90 minutes) and **commit working progress early** (right after R12a.1 works).

**R12a.1 BLOCKER — drag a hand card onto the board to play it.** Today every drag of a hand card only reorders the hand and nothing is summoned. New gesture rules (mouse, pen and touch alike, built on pointer events):
- A press on a hand card (raised **or** still tucked: pressing the visible tucked strip works without waiting for the raise) starts a drag after the pointer moves ≥ 6 px.
- **Play drag:** as soon as the pointer leaves the hand band upward, or has moved more vertically than sideways, the drag is a play drag: the rest of the hand tucks off the board (R11.9, shown fraction ≤ 0.32), the "Dragged card" (R11.8: real face, `data-card-id`, `data-tilt`) follows the pointer, and the legal squares for that card highlight (`getArena().highlights`). Releasing over a legal square does exactly the matching `legalActions()` action: creature → `summon` on that square (incl. Tunnel squares and Miner upgrades onto your own creature → `minerUpgrade`), targeted spell → `cast` with that target. Crystal cards and untargeted spells play when released **anywhere over the board** above the hand band (`playCrystal` / `cast`). Releasing on an illegal square, or back in the hand band, plays nothing and the card returns to its place. After a successful play the floating card disappears and the hand shows one card fewer.
- **Reorder drag:** only while the pointer stays inside the hand band and moves mostly sideways (R11.7 unchanged; AC264 still holds). A reorder never plays a card.
- The drag must work with real browser input (Playwright `page.mouse` down → several moves → up at the square's screen point from `squareToScreen(row, col)`), not only through test hooks. No `act()` call is needed.

**R12a.2 Hand layout.** When the hand is raised (hover or drag-reorder) at the default view (after Recenter, zoom 1), at 1920 × 1080, 1568 × 782 and 1366 × 768:
- **Never covers the board:** the top of every hand card button is at or below the lowest screen point of the player's home row (`max y` of the corners of `squareQuad(7, c)` for c = 1…13), 2 px tolerance. Pick the card size and/or the default board framing (still cover-fit, never stretched, no black bars) so a band below the board holds the hand.
- **Even fan, fully on screen:** every hand card button lies completely inside the window (left ≥ 0, right ≤ width, top ≥ 0, bottom ≤ height, 1 px tolerance); with no card hovered, the distance between neighbouring card centers is the same for all neighbours (± 4 px) and every card lies horizontally inside the window; each card is at least 120 px tall at 1920 × 1080 and 90 px at 1568 × 782 / 1366 × 768; no card is hidden behind the hovered card (each non-hovered card shows ≥ 35 % of its width).
- **Hovered card** enlarges upward (its button height ≥ 1.1 × a non-hovered card's) yet still obeys the two rules above; neighbours may slide aside (R9a) but never move off screen. The large top-layer pop-out preview (R8b.6), if shown, is a separate preview element and is not a hand card button.
- At rest the R10.9 quarter tuck stays.

**R12a.3 Owner card paintings (binding, used exactly).** Copy these files from `factory-design/goblin-art/` **unchanged (byte-identical), same names**, into `public/art/owner/goblin/` (served at `/art/owner/goblin/<file>`), list them in CREDITS.md as owner-supplied, and use them as the pictures:
| Owner file (1024 × 1536) | Used as |
|---|---|
| `goblin-miner-card-art.png` | Grubgrabber Miner (`goblin-scrounger`) card painting |
| `boneknocker-card-art.png` | Boneknocker (`goblin-pebbler`) card painting |
| `shiv-rat-card-art.png` | Shiv Rat (`shiv-rat`) card painting |
| `scrapsmith-miner-card-art.png` | Scrapsmith Miner (`goblin-smith-miner`) card painting |
| `grubnak-on-throne-art.png` | Grubnak's Commander portrait |
- `getArtInfo(id).picture` for the four cards = `/art/owner/goblin/<owner file>` (the old generated `/art/cards/<id>.webp` is no longer used for them; other Goblin cards unchanged). Every face of these cards (hand, Dragged card, opponent reveal, card close-up, Deck Builder grid/close-up, mulligan) contains an `<img>` whose `src` contains the owner file name, shown in the frame window (cover/fit, **never stretched**: displayed aspect = drawn aspect, R5a.2).
- Grubnak's portrait: the Choose Your Commander card "Grubnak Rotgut, King of the Junkpile" contains an `<img>` whose `src` contains `grubnak-on-throne-art`, and every other place that shows Grubnak's portrait picture (focused Commander backdrop, any HUD portrait) uses the same file. Sproingle and Borgo keep `/art/commanders/<id>.webp`.
- The other owner files in that folder (model sheets, assembled reference, wide art, throne sheet) are **not** used this round.

**R12a.4 Report:** screenshots at 1920 × 1080: a drag onto the board with highlights, the raised hand, and the 4 new cards (close-ups or Deck Builder) plus the Grubnak select card.

### Phase 1B, Round 11 (owner playtest of round 10: intro sound & music, main menu, highlights + settings, mines, pop-ups, hand drag & reorder, unit rings, fixes, Ranged 7) — done

Source: the "Round 11" section at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md` (binding), `factory-design/round9c/menus.json` and the GDD. Keep **everything** from Rounds 8a–10 unless an item below replaces it. **Build only these 17 items**; queued Menu Builder / deck builder / halls / card template work stays queued. Faction colors (owner, binding): Goblins main **#3ee06a** + highlight #b45cff; Gnomes main **#9b4dff** + highlight #3fa9ff; Dwarves main **#2f5fd8** + highlight #FFD700.

**R11.1 "Click to begin" gate, intro sound, continuous music.** Outside test mode, whenever the Intro would play, a full-screen gate comes first: container `aria-label="Begin"` with the Oreheart logo (`<img alt="Oreheart">`), the owner's background/fonts, and a plain-text button **"Click to begin"**.
