Clicking it (or any click/tap/key on the gate) unlocks audio and starts the Intro; the owner crash sound `snd:qoqhrj` (at the crash start, vol 2) and `thunder` (at 470 ms) now really play, and **Tense Cycle** starts on the Intro. In test mode the gate appears only with `?test=1&intro=1&begin=1` (so older `intro=1` checks are unchanged). Music then plays **continuously** through Intro → main menu → Deck Builder → Settings → Rules → Choose Your Commander and back, never restarting between menus, until a match starts (crossfade to `first-fight` as before). Test hook additions to `getAudioState()`: `music.startCount` = how many times the current track has been (re)started since page load (stays **1** while moving between menus); every `sfxLog` entry has `name` and `played` (`true` once the sound actually started on an unlocked AudioContext; headless browsers must still report `true` after the begin click — a muted/0-volume buffer counts as played).

**R11.2 Main menu exactly as menus.json.** The five main-menu buttons (Play, Deck Builder, How to Play, Settings, Quit) use the file's text styles exactly: color **#ffffff** (`rgb(255, 255, 255)` on the element holding the word), bold (≥ 700), italic as in the file (Play upright; Deck Builder, How to Play, Settings, Quit italic), the file's `display` font, same size for all five, shadow 0.8, hover color #FFF3B0 with a #FFD700 glow. All other text pieces on the main screen likewise use the file's color/font/size/bold/italic/shadow/glow/halo. No gold/yellow resting button text.

**R11.3 Square highlights = light from the ground.** Placement, move, mine and attack highlights are no longer flat fills: each square quad has a bright glowing edge/rim in the edge color fading inward to a mostly transparent center, plus a soft outer glow. Attack squares use the attack color (default red #ff2a2a) the same way. Test hook: each `getArena().highlights` entry has `color` (edge color, lowercase hex), `glowColor`, `style: "edge-glow"`, `edgeAlpha` (opacity at the rim, ≥ 0.6 at default strength), `centerAlpha` (opacity at the square center, ≤ 0.35 by default and always < `edgeAlpha`; equals the Inner fill amount × strength, so 0 with Inner fill 0), `glow: true`, `kind`. `fillAlpha` is no longer required.

**R11.4 Settings → "Square highlights" panel.** On the Settings screen a section (`role="region"`, name **"Square highlights"**) with labeled controls: **"Edge color"** and **"Glow color"** (`<input type="color">`, defaults = the player's faction main + highlight colors), **"Attack color"** (color, default #ff2a2a), **"Strength"**, **"Glow size"**, **"Edge thickness"**, **"Inner fill"** (range inputs, Inner fill 0–100, 0 = edges only), **"Pulse"** (checkbox), **"Pulse speed"** (range), a live preview square (`aria-label="Highlight preview"`) and a **"Reset to default"** button. Saved in localStorage (per player profile = this browser) and applied to every highlight in matches. Test hook `window.oreheart.getHighlightSettings()` → `{ edgeColor, glowColor, attackColor, strength, glowSize, edgeThickness, fill, pulse, pulseSpeed }`; `edgeColor`/`glowColor` are `null` while they follow the faction default, lowercase hex once picked; `fill` is the 0–100 number (default > 0). Reset returns all to defaults (colors back to `null`).

**R11.5 Mine click area = its own square.** The mine overlay button ("Crystal site, row R, column C") covers only the mine's own board square (its ground quad, at most 3 px outside its bounding box) plus the pick icon if that sits inside the square. The tall crystal/banner drawn over the square above never captures clicks, so a creature on the square behind/above a mine can be clicked and selected. Clicking anywhere in the mine's square still auto-mines with all ready Miners. New test hooks: `window.oreheart.squareQuad(row, col)` → the 4 screen corners `[{x, y}, …]` (CSS px) of that square; `window.oreheart.getSelected()` → uid of the currently selected / picked-up creature or `null`. Clicking one of your creatures in your Action phase selects it (its legal squares highlight); Esc clears the selection. `mineModels[i].screenBox` stays as a report value only.

**R11.6 Played-card pop-ups: opponent only.** Only cards the **opponent** plays pop up (`aria-label="Card reveal"`, `data-side="opponent"`, left edge, stacked, at most 3 visible, newest on top — R10.2 rules). Cards the player plays never show a reveal element (no `data-side="player"` reveal is ever visible).

**R11.7 Reorder the hand.** Press on a hand card and drag it sideways while staying inside the hand zone; on release it is inserted at the drop position. The new order is kept for the rest of the match (playing or drawing cards doesn't reset it; new cards are added on the right). Reordering never plays, discards or moves the card to the board, and doesn't change the game state except the hand order shown.

**R11.8 Dragging shows the real card.** While a hand card is dragged, a floating element `aria-label="Dragged card"` shows the **full card face** (contains the `[data-card-id]` face and the card name), never a grey/skull placeholder. It bobs gently and tilts toward the direction of movement with a springy feel; its current tilt in degrees is exposed as the attribute `data-tilt` (non-zero right after horizontal movement, never beyond ±15). It disappears when the drag ends.

**R11.9 Hand while dragging.** While a card is dragged out over the board (pointer above the hand zone), the remaining hand cards tuck down (each shows ≤ 32 % of its height) so they never block squares. While dragging within the hand (reordering, R11.7), the hand stays raised (other cards show ≥ 60 % of their height).

**R11.10 Unit rings.** The ring under each creature uses its side's faction main color by default (player ring = player's faction, enemy ring = enemy's faction) and turns grey (the "Moved color", default #808080 mixed by "Grey amount") once that creature has moved/acted this turn, until its owner's next turn. Settings has a second section (`role="region"`, name **"Unit rings"**) next to Square highlights with **"My ring color"**, **"Enemy ring color"** (color inputs, default = faction colors), **"Moved color"**, **"Grey amount"**, **"Ring thickness"**, **"Ring glow"**, **"Ring opacity"**, a live preview (`aria-label="Ring preview"`) and **"Reset to default"**; saved in localStorage. Hooks: `getRingSettings()` → `{ myColor, enemyColor, movedColor, greyAmount, thickness, glow, opacity }` (`myColor`/`enemyColor` `null` while following faction defaults); `getRenderStats().unitRings` → `[{ uid, side, color, greyed, thickness, glow, opacity }]` where `color` is the color actually drawn now (lowercase hex).

**R11.11 FIX — basic Miner death draws a card** (R10.5, AC247): when a basic Miner (Grubgrabber Miner `goblin-scrounger`, Gemsinger Miner `gnome-prospector`, Stonebreaker Miner `dwarf-miner`, the free Miner) dies by any cause, its owner draws 1 card; not when used up as an upgrade, not for Smith Miners. Card short text and `longText` of the basic Miners mention dying and drawing (AC255). *Note:* the round 10 versions of AC247/AC255 wrongly used `gnome-tinker` (Aegis Tinkerer, a Fighter) as the Gnome basic Miner; they now use `gnome-prospector`.

**R11.12 FIX — Commander facing** (AC230): Grubnak's yaw follows the owner's `turn` 146° exactly as in R9c.2 (facing = (cx: sin t, cy: cos t), +cy toward the player).

**R11.13 FIX — Change Commander** (AC206): on Choose Your Commander the "Change Commander" button is never covered by the deck pictures; it is visible, on top (hit-testable at its center) and clickable.

**R11.14 FIX — no 3D viewer on spell/crystal close-ups** (AC209): only creature and Commander close-ups show the 3D viewer.

**R11.15 FIX — Deck Builder text colors** (AC210, AC241): deck list card lines, the "Deck counts" summary and the deck count line are white `rgb(255, 255, 255)`; + / − unchanged; "Back" bold (≥ 700).

**R11.16 Outdated checks updated.** Checks that contradicted the round 10 rules are rewritten: AC10, AC25 and AC109 allow basic Miners to Tunnel next to any mine (the back-row-only checks now use Fighters); AC40 allows touching mines; AC48 allows Crystal cards in the Action phase; AC46 and AC96 raise the quarter-tucked hand before grabbing/clicking a card; AC174 expects the quarter rest (15–35 % visible); AC130/AC139 read the deck count line (either "N cards (50–100)" or "N of 50–100 cards"); AC243 checks the edge-glow look; AC244 checks opponent-only pop-ups; AC246 checks the own-square mine area. AC245 (200 seeded matches) gets a longer time limit; the build should keep `newMatch` fast enough (≤ ~1 s each). AC252 (mine decal/level report) is unchanged — the build must report the R10.10/R10.11 fields.

**R11.17 RULE CHANGE — Ranged range 7.** Ranged creatures attack up to **7 squares** away along the 8 straight directions (same row, same column or exact diagonal) and the shot stops at the **first unit** in the line, **yours or the enemy's** (creatures and Strongholds): it can hit that unit if it's an enemy, never anything behind it. Config `rangedRange: 7`. Update card `shortText`/`longText` (mention 7 and "straight line"; no "5 squares"), Rules page, AI, `legalActions`, `act`, `previewAttack` and the drag highlights.

**R11.18 Report:** screenshots at 1920 × 1080 of the begin screen, the Intro, the main menu, a drag with highlights, the Settings highlight and ring panels, a unit behind a mine selected and an opponent pop-up stack.

### Phase 1B, Round 10 (owner playtest of 9c: highlights, played-card pop-ups, mines, four rule changes, Crystal Flow, card close-up, hand) — done

Source: the "Round 10" section at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md` (binding) and the GDD. Keep **everything** from Rounds 8a–9c unless an item below replaces it. **Build only these 13 items**; the queued Menu Builder / deck builder / halls work (Round 9b and the queued parts of 9c) stays queued. **Faction colors (owner, binding):** Goblins main **#3ee06a**, highlight **#b45cff**; Gnomes main **#9b4dff**, highlight **#3fa9ff**; Dwarves main **#2f5fd8**, highlight **#FFD700**.

**R10.1 Drag highlights.** While a hand card or a creature is being dragged, every usable square is drawn with a strong fill plus a glowing outline in the **player's faction main color**, clearly readable on the arena art. Squares where the drop/move would **attack** are **red** (#ff2a2a, or any color with red ≥ 200 and green, blue ≤ 90). Test hook: each `getArena().highlights` entry gains `color` (hex string `#rrggbb`), `fillAlpha` (0–1, **≥ 0.45** for every highlight, was soft before), `glow` (true: outlined glow). `kind` is `"summon"`, `"move"`, `"attack"`, `"mine"`, `"swap"` or `"spell"`; every non-attack highlight uses the faction main color, every `"attack"` one is red. Ranged attack highlights follow R10.6 (only legal lines).

**R10.2 Played-card pop-ups at the edges, stacked.** Every played card still pops up (the `aria-label="Card reveal"` element, R2.13 rules for duration, pinning and the Card pop-ups switch) but no longer in the center: cards the **enemy** plays hover at the **left edge** (box left ≤ 6 % of the window width, right edge ≤ 35 %), cards the **player** plays at the **right edge** (right edge ≥ 94 %, left ≥ 65 %), each with a small margin, fully on-screen, vertically centered in the area above the hand. Each reveal element carries **`data-side="player" | "opponent"`**. Several reveals of one side **stack**: at most **3 visible** per side, the newest on top (highest z-index, `data-stack-index="0"`, older ones 1 and 2, slightly offset); a 4th removes the oldest. They never cover the board center (the point at 50 % / 50 % of the window is outside every reveal) or the HUD crystals ("Your crystal", "Enemy crystal").

**R10.3 Mines may touch.** Random mine placement keeps every other rule (3 mines, rows 3–5, corner-mirrored, fair, ≥ 2 from each Stronghold, center mine) but **adjacent / touching mines are allowed** (never the same square). The candidate set must include touching layouts so they actually happen: over seeds 1–200 at least one layout has two mines adjacent. Mine models never overlap (each stays inside its own square's footprint).

**R10.4 Whole mine site clickable.** The mine overlay button ("Crystal site, row R, column C") covers the mine model's whole on-screen bounding box (crystal, banner, pedestal, rubble and pick icon), padded slightly. Test hook: each `getRenderStats().mineModels` entry gains `row`, `col` and `screenBox: { x, y, width, height }` (CSS pixels, the model's projected bounding box). Clicking anywhere inside it (including the crystal tip near its top) = auto-mine (R8.6 tap-a-mine behavior).

**R10.5 RULE — basic Miner death draws a card.** When a **basic Miner** (`role: "gatherer"`: Grubgrabber Miner, Gemsinger Miner, Stonebreaker Miner; the free Miner too) dies on the battlefield (combat, spell, poison, any cause), its **owner draws 1 card** (normal draw: deck → hand, no draw if the deck is empty; the event log records `minerDeathDraw`). A Miner card used up as an upgrade (`minerUpgrade`) does **not** draw. Smith Miners and other creatures don't draw. Update `shortText` (contains "dies" and "draw"), `longText`, How to Play/tutorial text and AI evaluation.

**R10.6 RULE — Ranged in straight lines, range 5** (replaces "Chebyshev distance ≤ 2" everywhere). A Ranged creature may attack a target up to **5 squares** away along one of the **8 straight directions** (same row, same column or exact diagonal). The shot stops at the **first unit** in the line: it can hit that unit, never anything behind it. Units = creatures and Strongholds (attacking the enemy Commander = the enemy Stronghold square is the first unit in a line within 5). Empty squares and mine squares do not block. Knight-move or other off-line squares are never targets. Strike-back rules for Ranged are unchanged. Update `shortText` of Ranged cards (contains "Ranged"), `longText` (mentions "5" and "straight line"), Rules/How to Play, AI and `previewAttack`.

**R10.7 Crystal Flow into the HUD counter.** Shard streams use the side's faction main color with highlight-color sparkles and end at that side's **Flow crystal counter** on the HUD (the "Your crystal" / "Enemy crystal" piece), not the castle or bank. `getFlowStreams()` entries: `to` = **`"player-flow-crystal"`** or **`"enemy-flow-crystal"`**; `colors` contains the faction main color (lowercase hex) first and the highlight color; new `toScreen: { x, y }` = the end point in CSS pixels, inside that crystal piece's bounding box. Savings into the Bank (R9) still fly to the bank piece.

**R10.8 Card close-up twice the size.** Clicking a card (hand card view, a tapped creature's card, a pinned reveal) opens the close-up dialog (still `role="dialog"` named after the card, containing its `[data-card-id]` face) at **twice the previous size, fit to the screen height**: face height between **80 % and 100 %** of the window height, proportions kept, fully on-screen. **No scrollbar** anywhere inside it (text sized to fit: every descendant has `scrollHeight ≤ clientHeight + 1`) and **no "Close" text or button**. Clicking anywhere outside the card face, or **Esc**, closes it. ("Full explanation" stays.) The Deck Builder close-up keeps its R6a.8/R9c layout.

**R10.9 Hand tucks to a quarter.** At rest each player hand card shows only about **a quarter** of its height above the bottom edge (**between 18 % and 32 %** visible; the cost and name stay readable). Hovering the hand (the R8b.5 tight hover box) slides the cards up fully; while raised, hovering a card enlarges it (existing pop-out size). Touch: tap the hand to raise it. Card size is unchanged (R9a: 33.81 % of the frame height).

**R10.10 Mine ground decal, no ring.** The white ring/ellipse around each mine is removed. Instead a flat ground decal lies on the board under each mine: a soft glow pool in the crystal color plus a faint rubble/cracked-stone shadow, tinted in the owner's faction main color once a side owns the mine. The small income tag ("Mine flow, row R, column C") stays. Test hook: `mineModels[i]` gains `ring` (false) and `decal: { kind: "glow-rubble", flat: true, color }` (`color` = the owner's faction main color when owned).

**R10.11 Mines sit flat.** Every mine model is upright and level: `mineModels[i]` gains `tiltDeg` (≤ 0.5) and `baseY` (world y of its lowest point, within 0.02 squares of `getPiecePoses().boardY`); all mines have the same `height` (within 0.01).

**R10.12 RULE (for now) — Crystal cards any time.** Crystal cards have **no once-per-round limit**. `playCrystal` is legal for the acting player at any moment they can act: their own Deploy **and Action** phases, and as the responder in a Respond window (doesn't close it). Still free, Instant speed, never ends a phase or turn. Update card text, `longText`, Rules, AI and engine tests.

**R10.13 RULE — Tunnel any time.** Tunnel (the three basic Miners and every Tunnel card) works from turn 1 with no condition: the creature can be summoned onto **any empty space adjacent (8 directions) to any mine**, in addition to the back row. The "behind on mines" condition is removed; the hand card shows "Tunnel!" whenever such a space exists. Fix any case where a legal Tunnel placement fails (drag-drop, `legalActions`, `act`, AI). Desperate Plunder is unchanged (castable only while the enemy owns more mines). Smith Miners still never Tunnel.

**R10.14 Report:** screenshots at 1920 × 1080 of a drag with highlights (move + attack squares), played-card stacks on both sides, the close-up, the hand at rest and raised, and a Crystal Flow stream into the counter (AC256 saves them).

### Phase 1B, Round 9c (fix the broken arena, Commander pose, owner menus from the Menu Builder, main menu picture, Deck Builder items) — done

Source: the "Round 9c" section at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md`, `factory-design/round9c/` (owner menus file, pictures, sound, Menu Builder source) and the GDD. Binding. Keep **everything** from Round 9a and earlier unless an item below replaces it; **rules and numbers are unchanged**. **Build only 9c**: Round 9b (the in-game editor, `factory-design/round-9b-queued.md`) is still not built. Where this section conflicts with older text, **this section wins** (in particular it replaces the main-menu button list of 5.1 / R8.1 and the select screen's "Fight!" wording of R9.4 / R8b.12). New criteria: AC228–AC242; updated criteria listed at the end of this section.

**R9c.1 MUST FIX FIRST — the arena draws.** In 9a the arena never appeared: the arena effect builder ran a builder function for every effect of the owner layout (`/owner-layouts/final-draft/layout.json`), the 3 effects of type `flow` had no builder, the whole build threw and the error was swallowed. Fix:
- `flow` effects are drawn by the **Crystal Flow** system (R9a.8), not the arena effect builder. The arena builder **skips every type it does not build** (recording it in `skippedTypes`) and wraps each single effect build in try/catch, so one bad effect never takes down the arena.
- Arena errors are never swallowed: each is `console.error`ed (message containing "arena") and pushed to `getArena().errors`. If the effects fail, the swamp background and arena picture are still drawn without effects.
- All **57** owner effects show (54 arena effects + 3 flow streams), with the owner's per-type brightness/size/speed (`effectMasters`); the **2** foreground cut-outs work.
- The arena is drawn into one WebGL canvas marked **`data-arena-canvas`**; it issues draw calls every frame during a match.
- `window.oreheart.getArena()` gains: `drawn` (true once the arena picture has been drawn at least once), `errors` (array of strings, empty when fine), `flowEffects` (number of `flow` effects routed to Crystal Flow, 3), `skippedTypes` (unknown types skipped). `effects` lists all 57 entries with their `type` (the 3 `flow` entries included). Test mode: `newMatch({ setup: { arenaExtraEffects: [{ type, x, y }] } })` appends extra effects (used to prove an unknown type such as `"bogus"` is skipped and the arena still draws).

**R9c.2 Commander and stronghold pose (owner Game Editor values).** Each 3D piece is placed like the editor: an outer group at the piece's board point → yaw (`turn`) → scale → the model, which is first offset so its bounding-box **bottom-center sits at the origin** (`-(min.x+max.x)/2, -min.y, -(min.z+max.z)/2`). Values from the layout `pieces`: `pFort` cx 1.5, cy 6.5, scale 1.27, stretch sx 1.03 / sy 0.88 / sz 1.15, turn 137; `pCmd` cx 1.5, cy 6.5, scale 1.48, turn 146, heart on; `eFort` cx 11.5, cy 0.5, scale 1.29, sx 1.19, sz 1.56, turn −19, flip; `eCmd` cx 11.5, cy 0.5, scale 1.65, turn −15, flip, heart on. Size = Model Editor scale (Grubnak 1.54, stronghold 0.67; 1 unit = 1 square) × `scale` × `characterSizes` group sliders; stretch in the model's own axes before turning.
- **On the ground:** each piece's lowest point (after scale and stretch) touches the board surface at its board point. Commander and stronghold share the point, so **Grubnak stands on the ground inside his stronghold**, not on top. No vertical offset.
- **Upright:** no tilt or lean on any piece (the editor's `characterSizes.tilt` 25° is a picture-preview trick only and is **not** used; this replaces the tilt in R9a.6).
- **Facing:** board axes +cx = right along the player's near edge, +cy = toward the player/camera. Models face their own +Z. `turn` t faces the direction (cx: sin t, cy: cos t): 0 = toward the player, 90 = right, 180 = the far edge. Grubnak at 146 faces up-board and right toward the enemy corner (back three-quarters to the camera). `turn` **replaces** any automatic face-the-enemy/face-the-camera rule; look-back, impatient or point emotes may turn him briefly, but he always returns to his `turn`.
- **The stronghold stays put:** no movement, bobbing, turning, retreat or idle animation ever; only its health heart changes.
- **Grubnak plays Idle and his emotes in place** (laugh = Chest_Pound_Taunt, point = Call_Gesture, celebrate = Victory_Fist_Pump, surprised/hit = Hit_Reaction(_1), cast = mage_soell_cast, defeat = Dead) with the scepter in his right hand (R9a.6 values). Emotes never change his board position.
- Test hooks: `window.oreheart.getPiecePoses()` → `{ boardY, square, pFort, pCmd, eFort, eCmd }` where `square` is one square's world size and each piece is `{ cx, cy, baseY (world y of its lowest point), yawDeg (facing in the board frame above, same convention as turn), tiltDeg, leanDeg, matrix (its world matrix as 16 numbers) }`. `window.oreheart.playEmote(side, name)` (`side` "player" | "enemy"; names laugh, point, celebrate, surprised, cast, hit) plays an emote. `window.oreheart.focusCommander(side)` moves the view to a close-up of that Commander and stronghold (Recenter returns), used for report screenshots.

**R9c.3 Owner menus from the Menu Builder (binding).** Copy `factory-design/round9c/menus.json` into the game so it is served at **`/menus/menus.json`** (unchanged content), its pictures next to it under `/menus/`, and the owner sound `snd-qoqhrj.mp3` to **`/audio/menu/snd-qoqhrj.mp3`** (exact file, 98 253 bytes). The game builds its menu screens from this file (port the behavior and numbers of `menu-builder-source.html`; do not ship the builder). File format as in its `units`/`notes` fields: `screens[]` with `id`, `kind`, `bg {src, color, dim, vignette}`, `music`, `transition {type, ms}`, `intro {length, next, skip}`, `sounds[]` and `pieces[]`; `first` = the first screen.
- **Pieces** are placed at `x, y, w, h` % of a **16:9 frame** that fills the window (at 1920 × 1080 the frame is the whole window; other shapes cover-fit the frame as the menus already do in R5a.4, never stretched), with `rot`, `mirror`, `flipV`, `opacity`, `z`, `hidden`. Every rendered piece (text, button, pic, panel, zone) is one element with **`data-menu-piece="<piece id>"`** whose box is the piece's box. Text/buttons use the owner's `text` look (size % of screen height, color, font, bold, italic, align, spacing, shadow, glow, halo); buttons hover with `hoverColor`/`hoverGlow`. Buttons are real `<button>`s whose accessible name is their `content`.
- **Zones** mark where the game draws its own live content, sized to the zone box, with the zone's text look (`zone.st`), owner words (`zone.words`, e.g. `{deck}`), names and per-word overrides: Deck Builder `dFilters` (filters), `dGrid` (the card grid, `aria-label="All cards"`), `dList` (deck list, `aria-label="Deck list"`), `dCount` (`aria-label="Deck counts"`), `dActions` (Auto-fill, Save, Rename, New Deck, Play), `dDecks` (deck picker = the "My decks" carousel), `dSlider` (cards-per-row slider); Choose Your Commander `cCards` (the three Commander cards) and `cDecks` (the deck choice row with difficulty buttons Easy / Normal / Hard). Existing labels and behavior of these parts stay as before.
- **Screens and words** (from the file): **Intro** (`kind intro`, shown first): container `aria-label="Intro"`, the owner's crest logo crashing in, the tagline **"Deadly serious. Completely ridiculous."**, **"Click to continue"**, the "I AM A GAME DEVELOPER" dirt picture; a click anywhere skips it (`intro.skip`), otherwise it goes to **main** after `intro.length` (7000 ms) plus its fade. **Main menu**: the logo (still inside the page's h1 as `<img alt="Oreheart">`) and exactly five buttons in this order: **"Play"** (→ Choose Your Commander), **"Deck Builder"** (→ Deck Builder), **"How to Play"** (→ the Rules page, heading "Rules", which now also has a **"Tutorial"** button starting the tutorial match), **"Settings"** (→ the Settings page), **"Quit"** (a web page cannot close itself: Quit returns to the Intro screen). The old "Tutorial", "Decks" and "Rules" main-menu buttons are gone and the old "no other button may contain Play" rule no longer applies (the owner's "How to Play"). **Deck Builder**: title "Deck Builder" (heading), the zones above, **"Back"** (→ main). **Choose Your Commander**: title (heading "Choose Your Commander"), `cCards`, `cDecks`, **"Back"** (bottom-left) and **"Fight!"** (bottom-right; starts the match → mulligan; Round 17: the only start button, there is no "Start" button).
- **Intro once per tab:** outside test mode the Intro plays on the first load in a browser tab (remembered in `sessionStorage`; a reload in the same tab goes straight to the main menu); `?intro=1` always plays it. In test mode (`?test=1`) it is skipped unless `?intro=1` is given. The "Welcome to Oreheart!" offer (5.2) appears on the main menu after the Intro.
- **Animations** (`anim`): entrances none / fade / slide / grow / shrink / drop / spin / flash / wipe / **crash** (falls straight down from above the screen, squashes on impact at 60 % of `dur`, rebounds, dirt chunks and dust in `crash.color` fly from the piece's bottom edge, the screen shakes by `crash.shake`), with `delay`, `dur`, `ease`; loops float / pulse / glow / sway / blink / spin. Port the exact keyframes from the builder. Each dirt/dust element created by a crash carries **`data-crash-debris`**; they appear at impact time and are removed afterwards. Pieces end exactly in their boxes.
- **Sound effects** (`sounds[]` on pieces and screens: `{src, when (start | impact | end | open), at, vol, pitch}`): built-in names (crash, boom, bang, thud, whoosh, whistle, rumble, clang, shatter, pebbles, thunder, zap, sparkle, stomp, swordhit) are synthesized with Web Audio by porting the builder's `SFX` code exactly; `snd:<id>` plays the owner file. `start`/`end`/`impact` are relative to the piece's animation (start = after its `delay`), `open` to the screen opening, plus `at` ms. Volume follows the Sound effects volume and Mute. `getAudioState()` gains **`sfxLog`**: one entry per cue `{ src, screen, t }` (`t` = ms since the screen opened), recorded even when the browser has not yet allowed audio.
- **Music** per screen from the file (`tense-cycle` on all four menu screens), crossfades as in R9a.9.
- **Fonts:** bundle locally (like the Alegreya files) the Google Fonts the menus file uses (from Alegreya SC, Alegreya Sans, Cinzel, Cinzel Decorative, MedievalSharp, Pirata One, Metamorphous; SIL OFL; list in `CREDITS.md`). The file's `display` font means Alegreya SC Italic.

**R9c.4 Main menu picture.** `factory-design/round9c/main-menu-background.png` (1774 × 887) becomes the game's WebP at the same path as before, **`/art/menu-cavern.webp`, 1774 × 887**, and is the main menu background (the menus file's `menubg`). The element showing it carries **`data-menu-bg`** and is cover-fit (`object-fit: cover` or `background-size: cover`): no stretching, no black bars.

**R9c.5–10 Deck Builder.**
- 5 / 9: each saved deck entry in "My decks" carries `data-faction` (goblin / gnome / dwarf, from its Deck Commander); its name is tinted in that faction's color (Goblin green, Gnome blue, Dwarf purple; readable) and the entry has an outer glow in the same color.
- 6: clicking **any creature card** opens the close-up with the card on the left and the 3D viewer on the right (`role="region"`, name "3D model": drag to spin, zoom, a plain-text button for every animation the model has, at least **"Idle"**), using the board placeholder model for creatures without their own model. Spells and Crystal cards show the card centered alone (no "3D model" region).
- 7: single click on a saved deck selects it; **double-click starts a match** with it (its Deck Commander, Normal or the remembered difficulty) → Mulligan.
- 8: new plain-text action **"Play"** next to Auto-fill / Save / Rename / New Deck: disabled while the deck is not legal, with a short visible reason next to it that contains "not legal" (e.g. "Deck not legal: needs at least 50 cards"); when legal it starts a match with the deck (like 7).
- 10: deck list card lines and the "Deck counts" summary (and the "N of 50–100 cards" count) are **white** `rgb(255, 255, 255)` (owner exception to the text palette; the menus file wins where it sets a color); + / − unchanged; **"Back" is bold** (≥ 700).

**R9c.11 Report:** screenshots at 1920 × 1080 and 844 × 390 of the match at rest with the arena visible, each Commander close-up (at rest and mid-emote), every menu screen (intro, main, Deck Builder, Choose Your Commander) and the Deck Builder with a creature close-up (AC242 saves them).

**Updated older criteria (Round 9c):** AC1 (main menu buttons Play, Deck Builder, How to Play, Settings, Quit); AC3, AC123, AC171 and every test that opened Rules use "How to Play"; every test that opened the Deck Builder uses "Deck Builder"; every "Fight!" was "Start" (Round 17 reverts this: the button is "Fight!" again); AC25 reaches "Tutorial" through How to Play; tests that load `/` outside test mode first click through the Intro; AC51 no longer checks the turn display's position (AC212 does); AC59, AC164 and AC174 use the R9a.5 hand sizes (rest 31–37 %, raised 48–60 % of the frame height, about 1.6×); AC152 checks the Bank numbers on the bank pieces and the Commander names beside the crystals; AC171 allows the 3 Goblin corner frames on the match screen (AC215); AC179 expects the owner mine height 2.43–2.53; AC186 checks the 54 non-flow arena effects (the 3 flow effects are AC229); R9a.6's 25° piece tilt is removed (AC230).

### Phase 1B, Round 9a (the owner's match-screen layout, Goblin theme, banks, drop-down Menu, hand motion, 3D mines, Crystal Flow, music) — done

Source: the "Round 9a" section at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md`, the owner files it lists and the GDD. Binding. Keep **everything** from Rounds 8a and 8b and earlier unless an item below replaces it; **rules and numbers are unchanged**. **Build only 9a.** Round 9b (the Owner Game Editor inside the game, `factory-design/round-9b-queued.md`) is **not** part of this round: no `?editor=1` mode, no handles, no import/export. The owner's Game Editor page `factory-design/owner-tools/game-editor-source.html` is the **reference implementation** (layout drawing, `buildMineModel`, `makeFlow`, hand motion, drop-down menu, banks): port its behavior and numbers, do not ship the editor page. Where this section conflicts with older text (all R5b.4 / R6a.1 / R6a.3 / R6a.6 / R7.A2 / R8b.6 HUD positions and hand/pop-out sizes, the R5b.4 pop-out Menu and its hover behavior, the R6a.11 "no corner ornaments" rule **on the Goblin match screen only**, the R5b.4 bank badge on the crystal, R7.A5 / R8a.2 mine model and height, 5.10 "Surrender" wording), **this section wins**. New criteria: AC212–AC227; updated criteria: AC20, AC73, AC74, AC104, AC151, AC154, AC160, AC161, AC163, AC166, AC172, AC175, AC193, AC201.

**Owner files served by the app (byte-identical copies, needed by the game and by the tests):**
- `/owner-layouts/final-draft/layout.json` (the owner's layout, 34 481 bytes) and its pictures `/owner-layouts/final-draft/img-<id>.webp`.
- `/themes/goblin.json`: the Goblin theme (the owner's `goblin-theme.json`, may be wrapped/extended but must keep the owner's `faction`, `cornerFrames`, `flowCrystal` data) plus its pictures. One theme file per faction under `/themes/`; Gnome and Dwarf files do not exist yet.
- `/art/ui/goblin-bank.png` (2 570 231 bytes) and `/art/ui/opponent-bank-placeholder.png` (959 822 bytes).
- `/audio/music/tense-cycle.mp3` (1 540 211 bytes) and `/audio/music/first-fight.mp3` (1 667 679 bytes).

**R9a.1 Match screen = the owner's layout.**
- Every HUD piece in `layout.json` → `hud` (each has `id`, `kind`, `role` or a `name`, and `x, y, w, h` in % of its parent) is drawn where the layout puts it, at that size, rotation (`rot`), layer (`z`), opacity, with its text, font, color, shadow and glow; `mirror` / `flipV` flip only the picture. A piece with `parent` is placed inside that piece's box. Text `size` is % of the frame height × `textScale` (default 1). Pictures `img:<id>` are the layout folder's `img-<id>.webp`; `deck` = the round 7 deck pile art (`deck-pile`), `cardback` = the round 7 card back.
- **The 16:9 frame:** the HUD is laid out in a 16:9 frame. At exactly 16:9 the frame is the window. When the window is **wider** than 16:9, the frame height = window height and frame width = height × 16 / 9; each top-level piece is **anchored horizontally** by its layout center x: center < 33.33 % → frame placed at the window's left edge; center > 66.67 % → at the right edge; otherwise centered. When the window is **taller** than 16:9, frame width = window width, height = width × 9 / 16, and pieces anchor vertically by their center y the same way (top / middle / bottom). Child pieces follow their parent. Sizes scale with the frame. The HUD stays fixed while the board zooms and pans (R8a).
- **Every HUD element carries `data-hud-role="<role>"`** on its outer box (for pieces without a `role`, the layout `id`, e.g. `pic2`), and that box's unrotated layout rectangle is what `getHudLayout()` reports. Existing accessible names stay on these pieces: `player-flow-crystal` piece = `aria-label="Your crystal"` containing the Flow number `aria-label="Your Flow crystals"`; `enemy-flow-crystal` = "Enemy crystal" with "Enemy Flow crystals"; `player-next-flow` = "Your income next turn" ("Next +N"); `enemy-next-flow` = "Enemy income next turn"; `player-bank` = "Your Bank crystals"; `enemy-bank` = "Enemy Bank crystals"; `player-deck` = "Your deck"; `enemy-deck` = "Enemy deck"; `enemy-hand` = "Enemy hand"; `player-hand` = "Your hand"; `turn-banner` + `phase-list` + `phase-timer` together inside `aria-label="Current phase"` (the turn text still says "Your turn" / "Enemy turn"); `done-button` = the "Done" button (R5b.7 "only when needed" still holds); `menu-button` = the "Menu" button. Text pieces show live values (names, Flow, Next, deck counts, turn, phase, timer); the layout's sample values are only placeholders.
- **Highlights (from the layout):** your Flow crystal (green shard picture `img:prbig7`) large at the bottom-left with "Flow" / "Next +N" beside it; your Commander's name top-left; enemy name, "Flow", "Next", crystal (white shard picture `img:zrfak9`, placeholder) top-right; enemy deck and card fan at the top, **upside down** (deck `rot` 172°, fan `rot` −180°) facing the opponent, while the enemy deck count stays readable (its own counter-rotation −173° keeps it within 10° of upright on screen); turn, phase list, timer and Done grouped bottom-right; your deck bottom-right; the three **goblin corner frames** (`pic2` top-left, `pic3` bottom-right, `pic4` bottom-left) exactly as placed.
- **Test hook:** `window.oreheart.getHudLayout()` → `{ frame: { x, y, width, height }, pieces: [{ id, role, kind, rect: { x, y, width, height }, rot, z, src }] }` in window CSS px (`rect` = the unrotated box before rotation, `rot` = the on-screen rotation in degrees including parents, `src` = the picture URL shown or null). Includes every layout HUD piece that is shown now (Done may be missing when hidden).

**R9a.2 Faction themes (new rule).** The match screen's look follows each side's **Commander faction**, for the player and the opponent: corner frames, Flow crystal picture, bank picture and colors (text, glows, menu, card glow/halo, Crystal Flow colors). Themes are data: one file per faction in `/themes/` (only `goblin.json` exists now); new ones drop in without code changes. A side whose faction has no theme file uses the **placeholder pieces**: the white shard crystal (`img:zrfak9`), `opponent-bank-placeholder.png`, **no corner frames**, neutral gold colors. Corner frames belong to the **player's** theme (the screen's frame). The goblin corner frames are `data-ornament="corner"` elements: exactly **3** on the match screen when the player's Commander is a Goblin, **0** for a Gnome or Dwarf player; every other screen keeps 0 (R6a.11 unchanged there).
- **Test hook:** `window.oreheart.getTheme(side)` (`side` = `"player"` | `"opponent"`) → `{ faction, placeholder, crystalSrc, bankSrc, cornerFrames }` (`faction` = the Commander's faction id `"goblin" | "gnome" | "dwarf"`, `placeholder` = true when no theme file exists for it, `cornerFrames` = number of corner frames this side's theme draws (3 for a Goblin player, 0 otherwise and always 0 for the opponent)).

**R9a.3 Banks are their own pieces.** The Crystal Bank is no longer drawn on the Flow crystal (no bank badge or bank number inside the crystal pieces). Each side has its own **bank** piece (`player-bank` / `enemy-bank`): its faction's bank picture (`goblin-bank.png` for Goblins, `opponent-bank-placeholder.png` for any side without a theme) with the banked number on it as text (owner number style). The "+1 banked" savings animation (R9.2) flies into the bank piece.

**R9a.4 Drop-down Menu.** The "Menu" button (top-left, `aria-expanded`) **opens on click** (not on hover) a drop-down panel `role="menu"` named **"Match menu"** with exactly these items, in this order, each a `role="menuitem"`: **"Resume"**, **"Settings"**, **"How to play"**, **"Sound Effects"**, **"Background music"**, **"Give up match!"**. It opens downward, lined up with the button's left edge, words left-justified; animation "grow" with a springy ease (~260 ms), items appearing 50 ms apart; dark panel and item look/hover (gold glow, slight grow, slide) exactly as the layout's `menu` settings; plain text items, no boxes. Click outside, Esc, "Menu" again or "Resume" closes it.
- **Settings** opens the dialog **"Match settings"** (`role="dialog"`, Close button and Esc) holding the in-match controls that used to be in the pop-out Menu: **"Last turn"** (button; opens the R9.7 Last turn dialog and closes Match settings), **"Mute"** (button, `aria-pressed`, silences sound effects and music, remembered), the **"Card pop-ups"** switch (R2.15), **"Recenter"** (button; resets the view as R8a and closes Match settings), plus the two volume sliders below.
- **Sound Effects** opens a small panel with the slider **"Sound effects volume"**; **Background music** opens one with the slider **"Music volume"** (both `role="slider"` / `input type=range`, 0–100, remembered in localStorage, also present in the main-menu Settings screen and in Match settings).
- **How to play** opens the dialog **"How to play"** with the Rules page text and a Close button.
- **Give up match!** asks **"Give up this match?"** with **"Yes, give up"** and **"Cancel"** (plain text). Yes ends the match as a loss (Defeat, `winner` = opponent); Cancel returns to the match unchanged. (Replaces "Surrender"; the engine action is still `surrender`.)

**R9a.5 Your hand (port the editor's hand).** From the layout's `hand` settings: fan of up to 10 card slots, card width = hand width ÷ (1 + 9 × `overlap` 0.2), card height = the hand piece's height (**33.81 %** of the frame height), fan angle 4° per card, curve (outer cards lower). At rest the cards are tucked about halfway below the screen edge (the hand piece's top at 81.37 %).
- **Hover:** the card under the pointer **slides fully up out of the hand** (its whole card above the bottom of the window), straightens and grows to the pop-out size (`popScale` **1.6** × the resting card height ≈ 54 % of the frame height), eased ~**450 ms**; **neighbor cards move aside** (away from it); moving the pointer across the hand **shuffles through the cards** one by one: hover is decided by the pointer's x position over the hand's resting fan (the raised card never blocks its neighbors). The raised card **is the hand card itself** (no separate pop-out copy); still one raised card at a time, nothing stuck (R7.A1), never rising during other drags (R8b.5).
- **Glow and halo:** playable glow on every playable card (green `#9dff3a`, owner strength 0.6 / size 0.8, theme color), gold halo `#FFD700` on the hovered card; R8b.4 glow direction still holds.
- **Deal-in:** drawn cards (the opening hand and every draw) slide up from below the screen into the hand one after another: **700 ms** per card, **130 ms** apart.
- **Test hooks:** `getHandHover()` keeps R8b.5 fields and adds `raisedIndex` (index of the raised card or −1), `popScale` (1.6) and changes `popMs` to the raise duration (400–500). `window.oreheart.getDealLog()` → the deal-in animations started since the match began, in order: `[{ uid, startMs, durationMs }]` (`startMs` from `performance.now()`).

**R9a.6 Board pieces.** Place and size the 3D pieces as in `pieces` and `characterSizes`: Grubnak (1.54 × `scale` 1.48) on his stronghold (0.67 × 1.27, stretched `sx/sy/sz`), the enemy Commander and stronghold, the mines (fitted to 1.7 squares tall × `scale` 1.46 = **2.48 squares** tall), creatures (× 1.37), each tilted toward the camera by `characterSizes.tilt` (25°). The owner's `turn` values are the resting facing; R8b.3 facing/emote rules still apply (`facingDeg` is measured relative to that resting facing toward the opponent). **Grubnak holds his scepter**: the scepter GLB attached to the `RightHand` bone with position (−4.404, 8.752, −3.774), rotation (−109.365°, 5.199°, 154.757°) XYZ, scale 61.35, and he plays his Idle animation. Health hearts as in the layout (`heart`, `heartY` 0.12).
- **Test hook:** the Grubnak entry of `getRenderStats().commanderModels` gains `held: { item: "scepter", bone: "RightHand", position: [x, y, z], rotationDeg: [x, y, z], scale }` (null for Commanders without a held item) and `animation` (`"idle"` at rest).

**R9a.7 New 3D crystal mines (code-built).** Replace the mine model with the owner-approved mine built in code (port `buildMineModel`): stone rubble ring, iron-and-gold pedestal with corner posts and gold caps, blue banner with a gold diamond emblem, a tall glowing blue crystal with six smaller shards and a few on the rubble, a soft glow; `mineColor` #3d8bff, `mineGlow` 1, crystals gently pulsing. Ownership tint rules (AC172) still apply to the crystals/glow. No Meshy run; the Round 8a Meshy mine (`crystal-mine.glb`) is no longer used.
- **Test hook:** each `getRenderStats().mineModels` entry: `source` = `"code:buildMineModel"`, `height` (squares, 2.43–2.53), `owner`, `tint`, `parts` (includes `"rubble"`, `"pedestal"`, `"banner"`, `"crystal"`), `shards` (≥ 6), `pulsing` (true).

**R9a.8 Arena effects and Crystal Flow.** All arena effects of the layout (57 in `layout.json` → `effects`, with `effectMasters` per-type brightness/size/speed) replace the Round 8a list of 54. **Crystal Flow** (port `makeFlow`): a stream of tumbling faceted crystal shards snaking in moving S-curves from a mine to a target, with the owner's numbers (count, speed, arc height, S-bends, S-bend size, slither speed, spread, trail, end glow, colors). In play: when a side gains Crystal Flow at the start of its turn, one stream flows from **each mine that side owns** to that side's **bank piece**, in that side's theme colors (placeholder colors for sides without a theme), for about 2–4 s. The layout's three demo flows show the look (they are not shown during play). Drawn above the board, below the HUD.
- **Test hook:** `window.oreheart.getFlowStreams()` → `{ active: [...], log: [...] }`, each stream `{ side, from: { row, col }, to: "player-bank" | "enemy-bank", shards: "faceted", sBends, count, colors }`; `log` = every stream started since the match began. `getArena().effects` now lists the 57 layout effects.

**R9a.9 Music.** `tense-cycle.mp3` loops on the title/intro screen and every menu screen (main menu, Deck Builder, Choose Your Commander, Rules, Settings); `first-fight.mp3` loops during matches (including the tutorial match); **crossfade ~1.5 s** (1000–2000 ms) between the two when a match starts and when it ends (back to menus, including the match-end summary). Music starts after the first click/tap (browser rules), sits under the sound effects, respects Mute, and uses one **Music volume** slider; Sound effects volume slider next to it (R9a.4).
- **Test hook:** `window.oreheart.getAudioState()` (available on every screen in test mode) → `{ unlocked, muted, musicVolume, sfxVolume, music: { track: "tense-cycle" | "first-fight" | null, playing, gain, crossfadeMs }, log: [{ track, fadeMs }] }` (`gain` = the effective music gain now, 0 when muted; `log` = every track change in order with its crossfade duration). Audio playback errors in headless browsers must not throw page errors.

**R9a.10 Fixed test.** AC73's deck-memory test now saves a deck whose Deck Commander is Sproingle ("Gnome Rush") before choosing it under Sproingle.

**R9a.11 Report:** screenshots at 1920 × 1080 and 844 × 390 of the match at rest, a raised card with neighbors aside, the open menu, and a Crystal Flow stream (AC227 saves them).

### Phase 1B, Round 8b (owner playtest of round 7: Commander, cards, hand, deck builder, Choose Your Commander, text palette) — done

Source: the "Round 8b" section at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md` and the GDD. Binding. Round 8a is approved: keep **everything** from Round 8a (painted arena, view controls, Meshy crystal mine) and from Round 7 and earlier unless an item below replaces it; **rules are unchanged**. Items keep the owner's round 8 numbers (3–14). If Meshy fails (item 3), keep the current Grubnak model and clips, build the emotes from existing clips, finish the round and explain in the report. Where this section conflicts with older text (R6a.4/R7.A2 pop-out 85–90 %, R6a.7 "white body text", R7.B4 "View Grubnak in 3D" buttons and dialog, R8.11 compact stat pill look, R3.12 "other six starter decks" in the deck picker, R5a.7 Commander row spread, the R5a "last deck can't be removed" scope), **this section wins**. New criteria: AC196–AC211; updated criteria: AC66, AC72, AC117, AC144, AC146, AC164, AC167, AC174, AC183, AC184.

**R8b.3 Commander facing and look-back emotes.**
- **Default facing:** the player's Commander (Grubnak, and any Commander, model or placeholder) faces the **opponent** (up the board, away from the camera). The enemy Commander faces our side as now.
- **Turning:** smooth whole-body yaw turns lasting **400–600 ms** (eased), never snapping; he always returns to facing the opponent.
- **Look-backs** (turn to face the player/camera, hold about 1–2 s, turn back): at the start of the player's turn; when the player has nothing playable and should press Done (Done visible with no playable hand card and no creature that can act); and at random every 20–40 s while idle.
- **Impatience:** on the player's turn, after **20 s** with no player action he turns to the player and plays a frustrated emote (crossed arms, foot or scepter tapping, shrug), repeated, more annoyed (bigger / faster), about every **15 s** until the player acts. A "player action" is any pointer down or key press inside the match, or any game action by the player (including `window.oreheart.act`). Any action ends it at once and he turns back to the opponent. Not during the enemy's turn, Respond windows, dialogs or the match end.
- **Faces forward** (toward the opponent, cancelling any look-back) when acting against the opponent: casting a spell, his creatures attacking, laughing/pointing when an enemy creature falls, taking hits.
- **Animations:** use the saved Grubnak set. The builder may add **one or two** impatience / look-around clips **once** from Meshy's animation library on the already-rigged Grubnak (owner-approved), updating `grubnak.glb` and `models.json` (`grubnak.animations` may gain `"impatient"` and/or `"lookAround"`; `bytes` stays the real size; `creditsSpent` updated); never regenerate the model. If nothing fits, build the emote from existing clips (surprised + point at the player + head shake). The behavior is **data-driven**: one file `src/data/commander-behavior.json` (turn duration, look-back triggers and hold time, idle intervals 20–40 s, impatience delay 20 s and repeat 15 s, the clip used for each emote per Commander, with a default entry used by every Commander without its own).
- **Test hooks:** each `getRenderStats().commanderModels` entry gains `facingDeg` (0–180: the angle between the figure's forward direction and the direction toward the opponent's side, measured on the board plane; 0 = facing the opponent, 180 = facing the player/camera side for the player's Commander), `emote` (`"none" | "lookBack" | "impatient" | "forward"`, what he is doing now), `emoteLog` (emote names started since the match began, in order) and `turnMs` (the configured turn duration, 400–600). In test mode `window.oreheart.advanceIdle(seconds)` adds that many seconds to the idle timer (as if no action happened for that long); it is not itself a player action. In test mode the random 20–40 s look-back is off unless `newMatch({ randomLookBacks: true })` (so tests stay deterministic); the start-of-turn and nothing-playable look-backs stay on.

**R8b.4 Card glow direction.** Flip the glow: brightest and crisp right at the card's border, fading **outward** to nothing (no hard outer edge, no glow inside the card face). Applies to the playable-card halo in the hand and the popped card's rim. Keep R7.A4 thickness, opacity, colors, the `data-card-glow` element and its outset rule.
- **Test hook:** `getHandGlow()` entries gain `profile`: 5 numbers = the glow's opacity (0–1) at 0 %, 25 %, 50 %, 75 % and 100 % of the thickness, measured from the card edge outward. Required: `profile[0]` ≥ 0.8 × `opacity`, each value ≤ the previous one, `profile[4]` ≤ 0.1. If the glow is drawn with CSS `box-shadow`, it is not `inset`.

**R8b.5 Hand hover zone.** The hover trigger is a box drawn tightly around the hand cards: at rest only around their visible tucked part; while the hand is raised it covers the raised cards. It grows and shrinks with the number of cards and the fan width. Leaving it tucks the hand back. The hand **never rises** while a creature or board piece is being dragged, while the view is being panned, or during any other drag that did not start on the hand, even when the pointer passes over the hand.
- **Test hook:** `window.oreheart.getHandHover()` → `{ raised, zone: { x, y, width, height }, cards: { x, y, width, height } }` in window CSS px, where `zone` = the hover box now and `cards` = the union of the hand cards' visible on-screen rectangles now (clipped to the window). Required: the zone contains `cards` and each zone edge is within **16 px** of the matching `cards` edge (at rest and raised), for 1, 4 and 8 cards. `raised` = true while the hand is slid up. With an empty hand `zone` has width 0.

**R8b.6 Popped-out card.** (a) Smoother: eased scale and fade of **150–200 ms**, no jump or flicker; moving between neighboring cards glides the pop-out from card to card (R7.A1 rules still hold: one popped card at a time, nothing stuck). (b) **20 % smaller:** the popped card is about **70 %** of the window height (68–72 %; tests accept **64–76 %**), proportions kept, still on the very top layer and fully on-screen. `getHandHover()` also returns `popMs` = the pop-out animation duration in ms (150–200).

**R8b.7 Creature stat badges.** The creature label (same element, same accessible name "<Name>, row R, column C", same tap behavior) becomes a compact row of small **painted icons**, one per stat shown today: sword = Attack, heart = Health, boot = Movement, plus the same style for any other badge it shows (shield, poison, ranged 🏹 etc. may stay as small icons). Each number is drawn **on top of its icon** as real text (bold white `--text-white`, thin black outline, centered), so the label's text still reads Attack, Health, Movement in that order. Icons are original paintings made with the image API in the round 6a ruby-heart style, transparent PNGs saved as `public/art/ui/stat-attack.png`, `stat-health.png`, `stat-movement.png` (more allowed), shown as `<img>` (or CSS background) whose src contains `stat-`; listed in CREDITS.md. About **half the old footprint**: at 844 × 390 and zoom 1 a label is at most **76 px wide and 30 px tall** (it was up to 110 px wide), numbers at least 14 px; labels scale with the view zoom otherwise (never smaller than at zoom 1). Damaged Health number in `--color-damage`; values above the printed card value in `--color-success` (green). Hover or tap may show a slightly larger version. Badges stay above everything on the battlefield.

**R8b.8 Deck Builder: drag into the deck list.** Press and drag a grid card (ghost at the 3-per-row size, `data-drag-ghost`, R5a.5) and drop it on the **Deck list** to add one copy, same limits as "+" (dropping on the Deck pile still works). A press-release that moves less than about 6 px is a click and opens the close-up (R6a.8 / R8b.14); moving 6 px or more starts a drag and never opens the close-up. Mouse and touch.

**R8b.9 Deck Builder: no layout shift.** Adding a card by any method (double-click, drag, "+"), including when the deck list grows past its height and starts scrolling, never changes the size or position of the "Deck panel", the card grid (`aria-label="All cards"`, no visible label text, R6a.10) or the grid's cards (each within 1 px). Fix the root cause: fixed panel width, reserved scrollbar space (`scrollbar-gutter: stable` or overlay scrollbars) on the list and grid, fixed-width count and name columns, no width animation on new rows.

**R8b.10 Choose Your Commander: uniform deck entries and colors.** Every deck stack button in "Deck choice" (starter, saved, "+ New Deck") has the **identical size** (width and height within 1 px), border thickness and layout; all deck names the same font size; long names wrap or shrink inside the fixed box and never resize it. Selecting one glows; nothing changes size or moves. Colors (R8b.13 palette): deck names, titles, the Commander name and section headings bright gold `--text-gold` bold; "100 cards" and other secondary info `--text-brown`; actions ("Remove", "Copy to my decks", "Change Commander", "Back"…) `--text-brown-light` that glows bright gold on hover (Remove turns `--color-warning` on hover); Commander description text `--text-body` (#D4A94F).

**R8b.11 Choose Your Commander: picking a Commander.** Two states.
- **Row state:** the three Commander cards (`<button>`s named as before, `aria-pressed`) sit closer together (gap between card boxes 8 px – 6 % of the window width) but their glows and faded edges never overlap. The remembered (R9.8) or default (Grubnak) Commander is selected (`aria-pressed="true"`, strongest glow). Hover: the hovered card smoothly grows and the other two shrink slightly (≈ 200 ms, eased); nothing else moves.
- **Focused state:** clicking a Commander selects him and focuses him: the other two fade out and are removed from view (hidden, not focusable); the chosen card enlarges and drops back to a big dimmed backdrop (soft glow) in the screen center, and his **3D model** pops up in front of it (quick rise and scale-up with a small bounce, then idle with an occasional laugh or point) — for Grubnak the Meshy model on his fortress (if `models.json` exists). Commanders without a 3D model keep the enlarged card centered alone. The chosen card stays the button named after him (`aria-pressed="true"`).
- **Decks:** in both states "Deck choice" shows **only the selected Commander's decks**: his faction's three starter decks (Warband, Arcane Hoard, Horde, in that order, minus removed ones), then saved decks whose **Deck Commander** (R4) is him in save order, then "+ New Deck". Other factions' decks never appear. Saved decks keep their "ready / not ready" naming. The "Remove" action is disabled on the last remaining deck of the selected Commander (replaces the global "last deck" rule).
- **Back to the row:** a plain-text **"Change Commander"** button under him (only in the focused state), Esc, or clicking his card again returns to the row state (he stays selected).
- **Test hook:** `window.oreheart.getSelectState()` → `{ mode: "row" | "focused", commanderId, model: { shown, source, animation } | null }` (`model` null when no 3D model exists for him or in row state; `source` = `"grubnak.glb"` for Grubnak).

**R8b.12 Choose Your Commander: layout.** **Fight!** sits in the bottom-right corner (its box's right edge ≥ 85 % of the window width and bottom edge ≥ 85 % of the window height, fully on-screen), bright gold, bold, glows on hover, in both states. Back in a corner; Change Commander under him; Easy/Normal/Hard and the deck actions arranged cleanly with the deck row; all plain text actions in the palette colors; nothing overlaps; no scrolling of the page. **Remove** the "View Grubnak in 3D" button and its dialog from this screen and from the Deck Builder (no button whose name contains "in 3D" on either screen).

**R8b.13 Text palette change, everywhere.** Body, description and list text on every menu and screen (title, Rules, Settings, Deck Builder, select, tutorial and match dialogs, match-end summary) is brown-gold: add `--text-body: #D4A94F` (body on dark backgrounds) and `--text-body-small: #B8862B` (small or secondary), and change `--text-soft` to **#D4A94F**. Titles, headings, names and main actions stay `--text-gold` #FFD700. `--text-white` (#FFFFFF) is used only for numbers on icons (crystals, hearts, stat badges, deck pile counts) and text printed on card faces. Changed once in the shared theme file. On those screens no visible text containing letters, outside card faces (`[data-card-id]`) and `aria-hidden` parts, is white (#FFFFFF) or the old #F4ECDC; Rules page paragraphs computed color = #D4A94F. Check contrast at 844 × 390.

**R8b.14 Deck Builder close-up with 3D viewer.** Clicking a card in the Deck Builder opens the full-height close-up (R6a.8, still a `role="dialog"` named after the card) with the **card on the left** and, for a card with a 3D model, a **3D viewer on the right** (`aria-label="3D model"` region with a WebGL canvas, drag to rotate, scroll/pinch to zoom, soft lighting, plain-text buttons **Idle, Laugh, Point, Celebrate, Surprised, Cast, Hit, Defeat** (plus any added clip) that play each animation), side by side and centered as a pair on a dim overlay, both fully on-screen with small margins, no scrollbars. Today only **Grubnak's Commander card** has a model: the Deck Builder shows the deck's Commander card as a small card tile in a region `aria-label="Commander card"` next to the "Deck Commander" dropdown (a button named the Commander's full name, e.g. "Grubnak Rotgut, King of the Junkpile"; it follows the dropdown; it is not addable to the deck). Clicking it opens the close-up dialog named after him with the viewer (if `models.json` exists). Cards without a model show the close-up centered alone as now (no "3D model" region). Dismiss: click the card, click outside both, or Esc; dragging or clicking inside the viewer (including its buttons) never closes it. The card face keeps R6a.8 sizing (≥ 90 % of the window height, kept proportions) except that with a viewer it may shrink to keep the pair on-screen (≥ 70 % of the height). `getViewerState()` reports this viewer (`open`, `source`, `animation`, `rotationY`, `zoom`). No other viewer anywhere in the Deck Builder.

**R8b.15 Report:** screenshots at 1920 × 1080 and 844 × 390 of the Commander screen before and after choosing Grubnak, the Deck Builder close-up with the viewer, and a creature with its new stat badges (AC211 saves them), plus Meshy credits spent this round (0 if no clip was added).

### Phase 1B, Round 8a (painted Skullfang Arena board + view controls, crystal mine in 3D) — done

Source: the "Round 8a" section at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md`, the owner's arena files and the GDD. Binding. Keep everything from Round 7 and earlier unless an item below replaces it; **rules are unchanged**; the **HUD layout stays exactly as it is**. **Build only the two items below.** The other twelve "round 8" items (Commander facing and emotes, card glow flip, hand hover box, pop-out card size, creature stat icons, Deck Builder drag/no-shift fixes, Choose Your Commander changes, Fight! position, brown-gold body text, close-up 3D viewer) belonged to **round 8b** and are now specified in the Round 8b section above, which wins. If Meshy fails (item 2), keep the current glTF mine, do not ship `public/models/mines/models.json` (so AC193 skips itself), finish the round and explain in the report. Where this section conflicts with older text (R5b.1 camera numbers 51°/48°/107 %/16 %/12 %, the R5b.1 "board no wider than the window" fit, R3.1/R2.10 camera wording, the procedural battlefield / painted ground / mountains of the Round 1 and Look & feel sections, the "on-hold arena GLB" plan, R6a.12/R7.A5 mine source), **this section wins**. New criteria: AC185–AC195.

**Owner files (binding, used exactly, no redraw, no image API):** copy these four files **unchanged, same names** into `public/art/arena/` (served at `/art/arena/<name>`; spaces none): `skullfang-swamp-background.jpg` (1672 × 941), `skullfang-arena.png` (1672 × 941, transparent outside the arena), `skullfang-arena-layout.json` (name `"skull arena final draft"`) and `arena-editor-source.html` (reference only; it need not be loaded by the game). The game **loads the layout JSON at runtime** (no hand-copied numbers) and reads its `note` for the coordinate rules. List them in CREDITS.md as owner-supplied.

**R8a.1 The match board is the owner's painted arena.**
- **Look:** the match stage shows, back to front: the swamp background; the arena painting drawn centered at `arena.centerX/centerY` (background pixels) with `arena.scale` (its pixel size × scale); `arenaLook` (brightness add, contrast, saturation, hue degrees) applied to **both** pictures exactly as the editor does; every one of the **54** `effects` (6 flame incl. two green, 7 glow, 1 lantern, 11 waterfall with splash, 12 acid pool with bubbles and smoke, 6 banner, 5 plant, 1 water shimmer on the background lake, 5 firefly swarms), each with its own sliders and colors, ported from the editor's shader/particle code (same look; each effect keeps its own randomness, e.g. glows flicker independently); then the 3D pieces (creatures, Commanders/Strongholds, mines and their effects, flow particles, spell visuals); then the two `foregroundCutouts` (polygons in arena-picture pixels cut from the arena painting: the front fence row and the bottom-right post), drawn **over** any 3D piece standing behind them. Effects are **clipped to the painting** (the two acid pools past the right edge are cut at the painting's edge). This replaces the procedural battlefield, sky, mountains and haze. Low graphics (Round 1) may reduce effect particle counts but still draws every effect.
- **2D board, 3D pieces:** background and arena are flat 2D paintings; pieces are 3D and move over them. The 3D camera angle is fitted **once** so pieces stand correctly on the painted floor; it is never re-angled or orbited (zoom/pan below is a 2D view transform, not a camera orbit). The R5b.1 numbers no longer apply.
- **Squares, no grid lines:** draw **no grid lines** anywhere on the board. The 13 × 7 board maps onto the owner's four floor corners `boardCorners` (arena-picture pixels, order top-left, top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left) by a **perspective homography**, the same math as the editor's `drawGrid`: board point (u, v) in 0–1 with column edges at u = (c − 1)/13 and c/13, row edges at v = (r − 1)/7 and r/7, row 1 = far (top) edge, column 1 = left edge, (0,0)→top-left corner, (1,0)→top-right, (1,1)→bottom-right, (0,1)→bottom-left. A square's **center** is the homography image of u = (col − 0.5)/13, v = (row − 0.5)/7; arena-picture pixels convert to background pixels by bgX = centerX + (ax − arenaWidth/2) × scale, bgY = centerY + (ay − arenaHeight/2) × scale. Every square's center, click/tap area, drop target and highlight uses this mapping, so highlights sit exactly on the painted squares. Pieces stand on their square centers (a creature's label "<Name>, row R, column C" is horizontally centered within half a square width of its square center).
- **Highlights only when needed:** square highlights (playable/summon squares, move, attack and mine targets, the hovered square) are drawn as the homography quad of each square, and only while a hand card or creature is picked up / being dragged or selected, plus the single square under the pointer while hovering the board. With nothing picked up and the pointer over the HUD, no square is highlighted.
- **View controls:**
  - **Zoom:** mouse wheel over the stage zooms toward the cursor; pinch zooms on touch. Smooth, eased, 60 fps on a phone; smooth/mipmapped filtering (never nearest-neighbor).
  - **Pan:** drag on empty board or background (left mouse or one finger), right- or middle-button drag, two-finger drag on touch, trackpad two-finger scroll (wheel events with a horizontal component or `ctrlKey` false and small deltas may pan; `ctrlKey` wheel = pinch zoom), **arrow keys and W/A/S/D** (while the match has focus and no text field is focused). Dragging a hand card or a creature **never** pans (dragging near a screen edge may nudge the view that way).
  - **One picture:** painting, effects, 3D pieces, highlights and the creature/mine/site accessible overlays all move and zoom together (a 2D view transform of the whole match view, e.g. `camera.setViewOffset` / projection crop matched to the backdrop). The HUD (crystals, phase tracker, enemy hand and deck, Menu, your deck, Done, hand, hearts' HUD copies if any) stays fixed and does not zoom or move.
  - **Cover-fit, no black bars, never stretched:** at zoom 1 (fully zoomed out) the painting fills the whole window at its true 1672:941 proportions (scale = max(windowWidth/1672, windowHeight/941) CSS px per painting pixel). If the window isn't 16:9 the extra strip is off-screen and reachable by panning. The view can never show anything beyond the painting's edges at any zoom (pan is clamped). You cannot zoom out past zoom 1.
  - **Default / Recenter view:** zoom 1, horizontally centered; vertically the view starts at the painting's **top edge** (any extra strip is cut from the bottom), so both Commanders stay whole at every window shape (16:9, 16:10, 21:9, 4:3, 844 × 390). Re-fit live on resize (keeping zoom 1 and these rules when at the default view).
  - **Max zoom:** about **2.5 CSS px per painting pixel** (the zoom stops between 2.25 and 2.75 px per painting pixel).
  - **Recenter:** the existing plain-text **"Recenter"** item in the match Menu (palette colors) and a double-click / double-tap on empty board or background return to the default view with a smooth ease.
  - The R2.19/R5b.1 accessibility and hand/card drag behaviors stay; tapping a creature still opens its card (R2.14) at any zoom.
- **HUD and Commanders:** HUD positions keep their AC151 tolerances. If at the new default view a HUD piece would cover more than 10 % of a Commander's figure, nudge that HUD piece only within its AC151/AC166 tolerances; the board view is fixed by the painting.
- **Test hooks:**
  - `window.oreheart.getArena()` → `{ layoutName, background: { src, width, height }, arenaPicture: { src, width, height }, arena: { centerX, centerY, scale }, boardCorners, look: { brightness, contrast, saturation, hue }, effects: [{ type, x, y, clipped }], foregroundCutouts, gridLines, highlights: [{ row, col, kind, center: { x, y } }] }`: `layoutName` = the loaded layout's `name`; `src` = the loaded URL (contains `/art/arena/skullfang-swamp-background.jpg` / `/art/arena/skullfang-arena.png`); width/height = the pictures' natural sizes; `arena`, `boardCorners`, `look` = the values in use (equal to the layout file); `effects` = one entry per rendered effect in layout order (`type`, `x`, `y` as in the layout; `clipped` true when part of it lies past the painting edge and is cut); `foregroundCutouts` = number of cut-out polygons drawn over pieces (2); `gridLines` = number of grid line objects drawn (**0**); `highlights` = squares highlighted right now, `kind` one of `"summon" | "move" | "attack" | "mine" | "spell" | "hover"`, `center` = the drawn highlight's center in window CSS px.
  - `window.oreheart.squareToScreen(row, col)` → `{ x, y }`: that square's center (homography) in window CSS px in the current view.
  - `window.oreheart.getCamera()` now → `{ zoom, panX, panY, minScale, scale, maxScale, view: { x, y, width, height } }`: `scale` = CSS px per painting pixel now, `minScale` = the cover-fit scale, `maxScale` = the max-zoom scale (2.25–2.75), `zoom` = scale / minScale (exactly 1 at the default view), `view` = the visible window area in background-painting pixels (x, y ≥ 0, x + width ≤ 1672, y + height ≤ 941, width/height = windowWidth/scale, windowHeight/scale), `panX`/`panY` = the view center's offset in painting pixels from the default view (0 and 0 after Recenter). `screenRects()` stays (Commander figures and the board's four projected corners in the current view).

**R8a.2 Crystal mine in 3D (Meshy).** A build-time script `tools/gen-crystal-mine-3d.mjs` (like the Grubnak one, reading `MESHY_API_KEY`, never run by the game) makes one **image to 3D** job from `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Lummex/Starter/main/factory-design/design/references/crystal-mine/crystal-mine-front.png` (tall glowing crystal over a stone shrine with a blue banner on a rocky base): PBR, about 10–15k triangles, textures ≤ 2048. It saves `public/models/mines/crystal-mine.glb` (≤ 5 MB) and `public/models/mines/models.json` = `{ "crystalMine": { "file": "crystal-mine.glb", "taskIds": [..], "triangles": N, "bytes": N }, "creditsSpent": N }` (`bytes` = real file size). Generated **once**; later builds must not call Meshy unless the owner asks, and the running game requests no `meshy.ai` host. It replaces the placeholder mine everywhere: rendered height **1.47–1.53** spaces, 1 × 1 footprint on its square center, tinted by owner as now (player blue, enemy red, unowned pale; the crystal and banner colors and emissive glow swap), keeping the mine glow, the R5b soul-flow particles and the R9 swinging pickaxe. `getRenderStats().mineModels[i].source` = `"crystal-mine.glb"` (or `"crystal-mine-placeholder.gltf"` if Meshy failed). CREDITS.md lists it (Meshy-generated from owner art). Report the Meshy credits spent.

**R8a.3 Report:** screenshots at 1920 × 1080 and 844 × 390 of the match fully zoomed out and zoomed in (AC195 saves them), and Meshy credits spent.

### Phase 1B, Round 7 (6a fixes + Build B begins: Grubnak in 3D with Meshy) — done

Source: the "Round 7" section at the top of `factory-design/design/oreheart-phase-1b-brief.md`. Binding. Keep everything from Round 6a and earlier unless an item below replaces it; **rules are unchanged**. **Part A (fixes) must ship even if Part B (Meshy) fails: do Part A first.** If Meshy errors or runs out of credits, keep the current Grubnak figure, do not ship `models.json` (so the Part B checks AC180–AC184 skip themselves), finish the round and explain in the report exactly what failed. Where this section conflicts with older text (R6a.3 card height 38–54 %, R6a.5 generated card back and card-back deck piles, R2/R3b deck image `Deck_image.jpg`/`deck-image` on deck pictures, R6a.12 mine height 1.4–1.6), **this section wins**. New criteria: AC174–AC184.

**Part A — fixes**
- **R7.A1 Hand bug (top priority): no card ever stays popped.** Restore the smooth, quick 5b motion. Rules: (a) when the pointer leaves a hand card, that card returns to its place and rest size in the fan at once (animation ≤ about 250 ms); (b) when the pointer leaves the hand area, the whole hand tucks back to halfway (R6a.3 rest state); (c) **only one card can be popped at a time**: hovering another card retracts the previous one; (d) nothing may stay raised or enlarged after a drag (dropped, cancelled or dropped back on the hand), after a card is played, on touch release, when the pointer leaves the window (`mouseleave`/`pointerleave`/`blur` on the document or window), or when the hand contents change (draw, play, discard, mulligan). "Popped" means the card is shown at the R6a.4 pop-out size (≥ 80 % of the window height); "at rest size" means the R7.A2 hand-card height (≤ 47 % of the window height). The enlarged pop-out must not trap the pointer: hover is decided by where the pointer is over the fan, so moving to another card or off the hand always retracts it. Keep R6a.4 (popped card on the very top layer, `elementFromPoint` at its center returns it while it is popped).
- **R7.A2 Hand cards about 10 % smaller than 6a:** each hand card at rest is about **41 %** of the window height (between **36 % and 46 %** at every window shape, never stretched), with the same tucked-halfway rest state (35–65 % of each card visible), centering and half-card overlap as R6a.3. The pop-out stays 85–90 % (80–92 % accepted).
- **R7.A3 Owner art used exactly (no redraw, no image API):**
  - **Single card back:** copy `factory-design/design/references/card backs.jpg` unchanged (1184 × 1664) to `public/art/ui/oreheart-card-back.jpg` and use it on every individual face-down card: each enemy hand card and any face-down or flipping card (mulligan, draw, reveal flip). Each such element keeps `data-card-back` and shows that file as an `<img>` (src contains `oreheart-card-back`) or CSS `background-image`. **Delete the 6a generated card back** (no other `oreheart-card-back.*` file, update CREDITS.md: owner-supplied).
  - **Deck piles:** copy `factory-design/design/references/deck-pile.png` unchanged (1155 × 1313, transparent background) to `public/art/ui/deck-pile.png` and use it, as an `<img>` whose src contains `deck-pile`, for **both** in-match deck piles ("Your deck" bottom-right; "Enemy deck" at the top just left of the enemy hand, visibly smaller than "Your deck"), each with its cards-left number drawn on top of the picture, **and** for the Deck Builder "Deck pile", the deck stacks in the deck picker ("Deck choice") and any other deck picture. The in-match deck piles no longer contain `[data-card-back]` elements. **`Deck_image.jpg` and the old `deck-image` file are not used anywhere** (no `img` src or background url containing `deck-image` or `Deck_image` on any screen).
- **R7.A4 Popped card glow.** Keep the at-rest glow exactly as in 6a. The glow is no longer scaled with the card: a popped card's glow has the **same thickness in screen pixels and the same opacity** as at rest. Glow color contrasts with the frame: **Goblins purple** (blue and red both clearly above green), **Gnomes blue** (blue clearly above red and green), **Dwarves yellow-gold** (red and green both clearly above blue), **shared/neutral cards warm white-gold** (all channels ≥ 170, red ≥ blue). A subtle slow shimmer along the thin rim is allowed (not wider, not brighter); smooth at 60 fps on a phone.
  - **DOM hook:** each glowing hand card contains exactly one `aria-hidden` element with the attribute `data-card-glow` whose border box covers the full visible extent of the glow ring around the card face (`[data-card-id]`). Its outset (glow box minus face box, measured on screen on each side) equals the glow thickness in screen pixels. Non-glowing cards have no `data-card-glow` element (or it is `display:none`).
  - **Test hook:** `window.oreheart.getHandGlow()` → `[{ uid, cardId, faction, glowing, popped, thicknessPx, opacity, color }]` for every card in the player's hand, in hand order, where `thicknessPx` = current on-screen glow thickness, `opacity` = current peak glow opacity (0–1, ignoring the shimmer), `color` = the contrast glow color `"#rrggbb"` the card uses (reported even when not glowing), `faction` = `"goblins" | "gnomes" | "dwarves" | "shared"`, `popped` = shown at pop-out size now.
- **R7.A5 Mine height exactly 1.5.** Scale the glTF mine so its rendered height is **1.5** spaces (`getRenderStats().mineModels[i].height` between 1.47 and 1.53; it measured 1.607 last round). Everything else in R6a.12 stays.

**Part B — Grubnak, Goblin Commander, as a real 3D model (Meshy)**
- **R7.B1 Generation script, run once.** `tools/gen-grubnak-3d.mjs` (Node, run by the builder, never by the game) reads `MESHY_API_KEY` from the environment and uses the current Meshy API (docs at https://docs.meshy.ai) with the public reference URLs `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Lummex/Starter/main/factory-design/design/references/commanders/grubnak/<file>` (spaces URL-encoded): (1) **multi-image to 3D** from `grubnak-front.png`, `grubnak-left.png`, `grubnak-back.png`, `grubnak-right.png` (PBR textures, about 30k triangles); (2) **auto-rig** (humanoid) and add the closest library **animations** for the 8 canonical names `idle` (breathing), `laugh`, `point`, `celebrate`, `surprised` (react), `cast` (spell), `hit` (take damage), `defeat`; (3) **image to 3D** fortress from `fortress-front.png` (static, ≈ 10k); (4) **image to 3D** scepter from `scepter-front.png` (static, ≈ 3k). It downloads GLBs, downscales textures to ≤ 2048, and writes `public/models/commanders/grubnak.glb` (rigged, animated, ≤ 10 MB), `grubnak-fortress.glb` (≤ 5 MB), `grubnak-scepter.glb` (≤ 5 MB) and `public/models/commanders/models.json`. The key is used only by this build-time script and never appears in the shipped app. Later builds load the saved files and **must not call Meshy again** unless the owner asks; the running game makes no request to any `meshy.ai` host.
- **R7.B2 `models.json`** (served at `/models/commanders/models.json`): `{ "grubnak": { "file": "grubnak.glb", "taskIds": [..], "triangles": N, "bytes": N, "animations": { "idle": "<Meshy clip name>", "laugh": "...", "point": "...", "celebrate": "...", "surprised": "...", "cast": "...", "hit": "...", "defeat": "..." } }, "fortress": { "file": "grubnak-fortress.glb", "taskIds": [..], "triangles": N, "bytes": N }, "scepter": { "file": "grubnak-scepter.glb", "taskIds": [..], "triangles": N, "bytes": N }, "creditsSpent": N }`. `bytes` equals the real file size.
